L663: ## 7.6.1.5. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Pooled Analyses, Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2
L664: During the 16-week, vehicle-controlled period of Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2, TEAEs were reported at a comparable rate in the delgocitinib group and the vehicle group. During this period at least one TEAE was reported in 291 subjects (45.6%) who received delgocitinib and in 153 (47.4%) subjects who received vehicle. Severity of TEAEs was similar between the treatment groups, and the majority were considered mild or moderate for both groups.
L665: TEAEs reported in 1% or more of subjects are presented below by MedDRA PT in Table 27, by broad Office of New Drugs custom medical queries (OCMQs) in Table 28 and by narrow OCMQ in Table 29. Higher rates of the following adverse events were reported in subjects who received delgocitinib in the pooled Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 compared to vehicle: COVID-19 (11.1% versus 10.6%), upper respiratory tract infection (1.4% versus 0.3%), hypertension (1.1% versus 0%), back pain (1.1% versus 0.3%), and influenza (1.1% versus 0.6%). The 95% confidence intervals for these risk differences did not exclude zero for any of these preferred terms, suggesting that the observed differences are not statistically significant and may be due to chance. Furthermore, review by the clinical team of narrative summaries, including timing of onset, vital signs and ECGs, subject comorbidities, evaluation of potential alternative causes, and consideration of low biologic plausibility did not support a causal relationship between delgocitinib and these adverse events. Accordingly, none of these events are considered adverse reactions (including those with a \ge 1\% absolute difference in incidence: upper respiratory tract infection and hypertension), and are not proposed for addition to labeling. There were no ADRs that occurred in \ge 1\% in the delgocitinib group and greater than vehicle proposed for labeling.
L666: The review team evaluated narrative summaries for adverse events occurring in \le 1\% of subjects who received delgocitinib and not observed in subjects who received vehicle. The reviewers determined that the following events may have a possible causal association with delgocitinib, and these are proposed for inclusion in labeling:
L667: - Finger cellulitis: One subject developed “cellulitis of the left middle finger,” on Day 15 of delgocitinib. The AE began as mild (2 days) and progressed to moderate severity (15 days). The AE was considered possibly related to delgocitinib by both the investigator and the review team. The subject recovered with antibiotic treatment but without interruption of the IMP.
- Paronychia: One subject developed “paronychia (application site affected),” on Day 39 of delgocitinib. The AE was assessed as moderate severity and considered not related by the investigator. However, due to limited information, the review team assessed the event as possibly related. The subject recovered after 17 days of antibiotic treatment without interruption of the IMP.
- Bacterial skin infections (two events in two subjects):One subject developed “bacterial infection on hand dermatitis” on Day 1 of delgocitinib. IMP was discontinued after 3 days, and the subject recovered after 13 days with antibiotics and very potent topical corticosteroids. The AE was of moderate severity and
L668: NDA 219155
L669: ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L670: considered not related by the investigator. However, due to insufficient information, it was assessed as possibly related by the review team.
L671: - One subject developed “bacterial skin infection of hands” on Day 16 of delgocitinib. The AE was of moderate severity and considered possibly related by both the investigator and the review team. The subject recovered after 14 days of antibiotic treatment without interruption of the IMP.

L704: * Indicates that 95% confidence interval excludes zero.
L705: Treatment-emergent adverse events defined as an event that started after the first application of IMP or that started before the first application of IMP and worsened in severity after first dose of IMP.
L706: Duration is 16 weeks.

L710: ## **Local Tolerability and Dermal Safety**
L711: Subjects and investigators evaluated local tolerability by active assessment in Trials DELTA 1, DELTA 2, and DELTA 3.
L712: ## **Subject Assessments**
L713: Subjects evaluated local tolerability weekly through the end of treatment. Subjects rated the worst stinging/burning related to IMP applications during the previous week in an eDiary from Day 7 onwards with a 4-point scale (“0 = no stinging or burning, 1 mild = slight warm, tingling sensation, not really bothersome, 2 moderate = definitive warm, tingling sensations, that is somewhat bothersome, 3 severe = hot, tingling/stinging sensation that has caused definite discomfort”). The assessment of local tolerability by the subject was reported as an AE at the discretion of the investigator, regardless of suspected relatedness of the local skin reaction to the IMP.
L714: In the primary pool, subjects who received active and vehicle reported similar rates of no or mild stinging/burning (80.7% and 73.6% respectively) at the end of Week 1, and more subjects on vehicle (11.0%) reported severe stinging/burning compared to active (4.9%). As the trials progressed, local tolerability for subjects in both groups improved, and by Week 16 approximately 90% of subjects in both treatment groups had no stinging or burning. Severe stinging/burning led to early termination in 3.2% of subjects on active and 8.9% of subjects on vehicle. For subjects who enrolled in open-label Trial DELTA 3 for an additional 36 weeks of treatment, tolerability gradually improved over time (subjects on active with no or mild stinging increased from 83.9% at Week 1 to 90.2% at Week 36).
L715: There were no differences in local tolerability reactions in subjects by primary CHE subtype (allergic contact dermatitis, irritant contact dermatitis, contact urticaria/protein contact dermatitis, atopic hand eczema, vesicular hand eczema pompholyx, hyperkeratotic eczema) within or across treatment groups.
L716: ## **Investigator Assessments**
L717: In Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2, investigators assessed local tolerability weekly and at the end of treatment. In Trial DELTA 3, assessments were performed every 4 weeks and at treatment completion. Active assessments for local tolerability were not performed for each subject using separate scales. Instead, investigators performed targeted evaluations when local skin reactions were suspected to be related to the IMP. In such cases, reactions were scored using the Berger and Bowman scale, which evaluates local skin signs including irritation, erythema, papules, edema, vesicular eruption, glazed appearance, fissures, exudates, and scabs. Investigators were instructed to assess only signs specifically attributable to the IMP, excluding those related to underlying CHE. The suspected etiology of the reaction was recorded in the electronic case report form as one of the following:
L718: - Irritant contact dermatitis caused by IMP
- Allergic contact dermatitis caused by IMP (confirmation required a positive IMP patch test)
- Other (specify)
L719: If a reaction met criteria for an AE, the final diagnosis was captured accordingly.
L720: ## Patch Test Findings by Trial
L721: ## Trial DELTA 1
L722: Six subjects experienced suspected local skin reactions and underwent patch testing (2 on active, 4 on vehicle). All scored results were negative, except for one vehicle-treated subject who did not return for the second reading. All AEs originating from patch testing were coded with PTs related to eczema.
L723: ## Trial DELTA 2
L724: Nine subjects had suspected local skin reactions; five underwent patch testing (1 on active, 4 on vehicle). Four subjects had negative scores at both readings. One vehicle-treated subject had a weak positive reaction at the first reading and a doubtful result at the second. AEs included one event of “application site erythema” in the active arm; all events in the vehicle arm were related to eczema.
L725: ## Trial DELTA 3
L726: Three active-treated subjects had suspected local reactions; two underwent patch testing. One had a negative score (one reading only), and one had a doubtful result. AEs reported following patch testing included “application site erythema” and “contact dermatitis.”
L727: Given the low number of positive or equivocal patch test results, the predominance of negative findings, and the frequency of reactions in vehicle-treated subjects, the overall assessment of skin irritation and sensitization via patch testing did not indicate allergic contact dermatitis related to delgocitinib.
L728: ## Adverse Reactions in &lt;1% of Subjects
L729: The review team analyzed application site TEAEs that were reported in &lt;1% of subjects in Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2. The following AE preferred terms were reported in &lt;1% of subjects, were considered probably or possibly related to delgocitinib by both investigators and the review team, and are proposed for inclusion in labeling:
L730: - Application site pain (0.6%)
- Application site paresthesia (0.4%)
- Application site pruritus (0.1%)
- Application site erythema (0.1%)
L731: ## Long-Term Safety
L732: The review team analyzed adverse events for subjects who received continuous and intermittent delgocitinib treatment over 52 weeks in the vehicle-controlled Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 and the single-arm open-label long-term extension Trial DELTA 3.
L733: There were no increases in the rates of SAEs or AEs leading to withdrawal from the trial and/or permanent discontinuation of IMP with long-term intermittent or continuous treatment for 52 weeks. In addition, there were no increases over time in the rates of severe AEs, which were similar between intermittent or continuous treatment groups. The distribution pattern of AEs
L734: across SOCs and PTs was generally similar across treatment groups and pools (Primary pool and Long-term safety pool/Intermittent-use pool). The review team identified no clinically relevant differences.
L735: No new safety signals were identified based on FDA broad and narrow medical queries and EAIRs (refer to Table 30 and Table 31). Analysis of vital signs, ECGs, laboratory findings and shift tables for subjects continuously treated for 52 weeks (refer to Section 7.6.1.6) did not identify any new safety signals with long-term use of delgocitinib for 52 weeks.
L736: While the current long-term safety database is sufficient to support approval, it has limitations in detecting rare or delayed adverse events. The conduct of a long-term, vehicle-controlled trial was not pursued by the Applicant due to ethical concerns related to prolonged use of vehicle in subjects with moderate to severe CHE with inadequate response to topical corticosteroids and the potential for high dropout rates which could bias results. A total of 638 subjects received 16 weeks of treatment, but fewer than 200 were treated continuously for 52 weeks. Trial DELTA 3, the long-term extension trial, employed an open-label, as-needed design to reflect real-world clinical practice. As a result, vehicle-controlled data are limited to the 16-week parent trials (Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2). Given the potential for dose-dependent, rare, and long-latency adverse events related to JAKi and the limited long-term safety database, the review team recommends appropriate class labeling to inform prescribers and patients of risks not fully evaluated in the delgocitinib clinical program. Refer to Section 7.2 of this review for the review team's rationale for inclusion of a Boxed Warning.

L768: ## 7.6.1.6. Laboratory Findings, Pooled Analyses, Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2
L769: Blood sampling for laboratory assessments was performed at Weeks 0, 2, 4, 8, 12, and 16 in Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2. There were no significant differences in laboratory findings (chemistry, kidney function, or liver biochemistry) between the delgocitinib and vehicle groups in Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 (refer to Table 32, Table 33, and Table 34 below). The 95% confidence intervals for these risk differences did not exclude zero for any of these preferred terms, suggesting that any observed differences are not statistically significant and may be due to chance.

L803: ## **Assessment of Hypoglycemia in Patients With Diabetes**
L804: Nonclinical data from the literature suggest that JAK and TYK2 inhibitors may cause hypoglycemia in diabetics. The authors postulate that JAK and TYK2 inhibitors protect pancreatic \beta-cells from immune-mediated destruction and improve insulin sensitivity, thereby enhancing endogenous insulin production and action (Gurzov et al. 2016; Bako et al. 2019). Product labeling for oral JAK inhibitors (e.g., tofacitinib, baricitinib, and upadacitinib) in the European Union informs prescribers that there have been reports of hypoglycemia following initiation of JAK inhibitors, and dose adjustment of antidiabetic medicinal products may be necessary in the event that hypoglycemia occurs.
L805: In the clinical trials, incidence rates of TEAEs of hypoglycemia or blood glucose decreased associated with delgocitinib 2% exposure (MedDRA search terms: hypoglycemia (SMQ) narrow search, blood glucose abnormal (PT), and blood glucose fluctuation (PT) were 0% among subjects with known diabetes who received delgocitinib or vehicle and 0.2% among those without diabetes who received vehicle (Applicant response to information request dated May 29, 2025).
L806: ## **Lipids**
L807: Lipid parameters were measured in a nonfasted state during the phase 3 trials. The review team analyzed values exceeding specified levels (refer to Table 35 below). There was a numerical difference in the number of subjects who developed high low-density lipoprotein (LDL) [Level 3 (190 mg/dL)] in the delgocitinib group (11.5%) compared with the vehicle group (7.0%) with a 4.5% risk difference. However, analysis of shift tables and subject narratives did not support a safety signal. Additionally, the collection of lipid parameters in a nonfasted state limits the interpretability of these findings and precludes meaningful clinical assessment of lipid changes. Table
L808: Shift tables and discussion of all lipid parameter TEAEs follow Table 35 below.

L818: ## Shift Tables and TEAEs for Lipid Parameters
L819: ## LDL
L820: The LDL shift table from baseline to worst (high) value for the pooled Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 (active and vehicle) to Week 16 is shown below in Table 36. One subject (0.7%) who received delgocitinib shifted from normal baseline LDL value to a very high (&gt;4.9 mmol/L) worst postbaseline value. The subject decreased from “high” (4.8 mmol/L) LDL levels at screening to “normal” levels at baseline (2.4 mmol/L) to “very high” (5.1 to 5.6 mmol/L) levels at Weeks 2 to 12. At Week 16, the LDL level returned to “high” (4.8 mmol/L). Shifts for other baseline grades to worst baseline levels were comparable between subjects who received delgocitinib versus vehicle. A total of 6 subjects who received 52 weeks of continuous exposure to delgocitinib shifted from borderline high baseline value to very high worst postbaseline value.
L821: In the primary safety pool to Week 16, AEs of “low-density lipoprotein increased” were reported with delgocitinib (6 subjects, 0.8%, EAIR 2.72) and none with vehicle (0%). In the long-term safety pool, AEs of “low-density lipoprotein increased” were reported in 1% (nine subjects, EAIR 1.49) of subjects who received delgocitinib and in 0.1% (1 subject, EAIR 0.45) of subjects who received vehicle or were off treatment. None of the events were serious, severe, or led to discontinuation or withdrawal from the trials and none were considered related by the reviewer.

L852: ## Cholesterol
L853: ## *CTCAE Grade Shifts in Cholesterol Levels*
L854: The cholesterol shift table from baseline to worst (high) value for the pooled Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 (active and vehicle) to Week 16 is shown below in Table 40. Overall, similar proportions of subjects were observed to have abnormal cholesterol levels (&gt;Grade 0) in the delgocitinib and vehicle groups. No subjects treated with delgocitinib in the pooled Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 shifted to cholesterol levels above the Grade 3 threshold of &gt;10.34 mmol/L. The cholesterol shift table from baseline to worst (high) value for subjects continuously treated with delgocitinib for 52 weeks did not reveal any new trend. No subjects had cholesterol levels above the Grade 3 threshold of &gt;10.34 mmol/L with continuous use of delgocitinib.
L855: ## *Adverse Events*
L856: In the primary safety pool to Week 16, AEs of “hypercholesterolaemia” were reported in 0.7% (five subject, EAIR 2.24) of subjects who received delgocitinib and in 0.8% (three subjects, EAIR 2.79) of subjects who received vehicle. Adverse events of “blood cholesterol increased” were reported in 0.1% (1 subject, EAIR 0.45) of subjects who received delgocitinib and no subjects who received vehicle. In the long-term safety pool, AEs of “blood cholesterol increased” were reported in 0.3% (three subjects, EAIR 0.50) of subjects who received delgocitinib and in 0.2% (2 subjects, EAIR 0.9) of subjects who received vehicle or were off treatment. Adverse events of “hypercholesterolaemia” were reported in 1% (9 subjects, EAIR 1.66) of subjects who received delgocitinib and in 0.3% (3 subjects, EAIR 1.36) of subjects who received vehicle or were off treatment. None of the events were serious, severe, or led to discontinuation or withdrawal from the trials, and none were considered related by the reviewer.

L917: ## Adverse Events for Subjects Exposed for 16 Weeks
L918: In the vehicle-controlled 16-week period of Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2, two AEs of anemia were reported in two subjects who received delgocitinib cream (incidence rate [IR]: 1.02), while no events were reported in subjects who received vehicle.
L919: - “Anemia” (Grade 1 decrease in hemoglobin, reported term: secondary anemia, Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6)).
L920: A 33-year-old male with baseline hemoglobin 145 g/L developed anemia (hemoglobin 127 g/L) on Day 43, 10 days after a COVID-19 infection. He developed COVID-19 interstitial pneumonia on Day 33. Hemoglobin levels subsequently trended upward until the end of treatment, when his hemoglobin level was 144 g/L. Plasma delgocitinib concentrations trended downward throughout the trial. Intermittent exposure to delgocitinib in Trial DELTA 3 showed no trend in hemoglobin levels during “on-treatment” periods compared with “off-treatment” periods. His final hemoglobin level at the end of Trial DELTA 3 was 157 g/L. There was no change to IMP administration. The AE was considered unrelated to study drug by investigator. The clinical reviewer concurs. The decrease was transient, and there was a lack of hemoglobin fluctuations during intermittent delgocitinib treatment (i.e., simulating challenge/dechallenge conditions) in the subsequent trial.
L921: - “Normocytic anemia” (Subject [REDACTED] (b) (6)): isolated decrease in hemoglobin [Grade 1]).
L922: A 51-year-old male with history of Factor V Leiden deficiency developed decreased hemoglobin from 168 g/L at baseline to 138 g/L at Day 85. At the end of the trial, the hemoglobin level had normalized to 162 g/L. No PK assessments were obtained. After the subject entered Trial DELTA 3 and received continuous delgocitinib, he developed no further decrease in hemoglobin. The investigator deemed the event unrelated to the study drug. The clinical reviewer concurs, in view of the isolated and transient nature of the AE,
L923: spontaneous resolution, and lack of continued drop in hemoglobin despite continued drug exposure in the subsequent trial.

L938: A narrative summary of the subject who experienced a Grade 0 to 2 shift in hemoglobin with 52 continuous weeks of treatment is below:
L939: - A 22-year-old female (b)(6) with baseline hemoglobin of 117 g/L developed a Grade 1 shift in hemoglobin to 111 g/L on Day 112. On Day 162 of continuous delgocitinib treatment, the subject developed decreased hemoglobin to 98 g/L (Grade 2). The subject's hemoglobin values fluctuated throughout the remainder of the long-term safety trial with a final hemoglobin level of 96 g/L on Day 364.
L940: Because no iron studies were performed, a definite diagnosis for the hemoglobin decrease is not possible. Because no PK samples were obtained, it is not possible to evaluate the relationship between systemic exposure to delgocitinib and the onset of this AE. However, there was no associated AE or shifts in any other hematologic parameters in either Trial DELTA 1 or DELTA 3 with continuous treatment through Day 364 to suggest broad bone marrow suppression. Other hematologic parameters were: baseline lymphocytes 3.9 in trial DELTA 1 and 3.4 at the end of Trial DELTA 3; baseline neutrophils 4 in trial DELTA 1 and unchanged at the end of Trial DELTA 3, baseline thrombocytes 437 in trial DELTA 1 and 432 at the end of Trial DELTA 3, baseline leukocytes 9.7 in trial DELTA 1 and 8.4 (no shift in grade) at the end of Trial DELTA 3. Therefore, the clinical reviewer considers the hemoglobin Grade 0 to 2 shift unlikely to be related to delgocitinib.

L944: whether PK sampling was performed, and investigator-assessed causality), refer to Section 17.1 Clinical Safety: Additional Information.
L945: A total of two subjects in the delgocitinib cream group had thrombocyte counts below 75\times 10^9/L (Grade 2), including one subject with thrombocyte counts below 50\times 10^9/L (Grade 3). Both subjects had a medical history of low platelet counts that confounded the assessment (both subjects with low platelet counts from baseline and throughout the trials). Brief narrative summaries are below:
L946: - Subject [REDACTED](b)(6): A 53-year-old female with a history of “thrombopenic” purpura and an active smoker with a baseline platelet count of 82\times 10^9/L developed thrombocytopenia on Day 85 (platelets decreased to 69\times 10^9/L (Grade 2)). At the end of trial, platelet levels slightly increased to 79\times 10^9/L. PK sampling was not performed. After completion of Trial DELTA 1, the subject received continuous treatment with delgocitinib for 52 weeks in Trial DELTA 3, and platelets trended upward. The final platelet count was 147\times 10^9/L at the end of trial. There were no shifts in any other hematology parameters with continuous treatment through 52 weeks to suggest broad bone marrow suppression. The clinical reviewer considers the Grade 2 platelet shift unlikely related delgocitinib.
- Subject [REDACTED](b)(6): A 36-year-old female with a history of idiopathic thrombocytopenic purpura and a screening platelet count of 60\times 10^9/L (Grade 2) and a baseline platelet count of 79\times 10^9/L developed thrombocytopenia on Day 111 (platelet count decreased to 38\times 10^9/L (Grade 3)). No further laboratory data are available after this date since the subject did not participate in the long-term safety trial. Plasma concentrations of delgocitinib fluctuated throughout the trial, though tended to trend downward over time [plasma delgocitinib level 0.48\text{ ng/mL} Day 8 \to 0.09\text{ ng/mL} Day 32 (0.112\text{ ng/mL} Day 111)]. The subject developed no concurrent adverse event or shifts in any other hematology parameters in Trial DELTA 2 to suggest broad bone marrow suppression. No follow-up laboratory data are available to determine if the subject returned to her baseline platelet count. However, given the medical history of immune thrombocytopenic purpura and the lower delgocitinib exposure at Day 111 than Day 8, the clinical reviewer considers it unlikely that the platelet shift was related to delgocitinib.
L947: In the primary safety pool, AEs of “thrombocytopenia” were reported in 0.3% (one subject, EAIR 1.94) of subjects who received vehicle, and no subjects who received delgocitinib.

L972: ## Adverse Events for Subjects Exposed for 16 Weeks
L973: In the primary safety pool to Week 16, AEs of “leukopenia” were reported in 0.1% (one subject, EAIR 0.46) of subjects who received delgocitinib (considered possibly related by investigator, narrative below), and none in vehicle.
L974: A 64-year-old female (b)(6) with a history of leukopenia, hypothyroid, and hyperlipidemia developed leukopenia on Day 113 of delgocitinib. The subject had baseline leukocytes 3.4 \times 10^9, lymphocytes 0.6 \times 10^9/L, and neutrophils 2.3 \times 10^9/L. The subject developed an AE of influenza on Day 109 and an AE of leukopenia (considered possibly related by investigator) on Day 113. The AE of influenza coincided with a leukocyte decrease to 2.5 \times 10^9/L (shift from baseline Grade 1 to Grade 2), lymphocyte decrease to 0.4 \times 10^9/L, and neutrophil decrease to 1.5 \times 10^9/L. Plasma delgocitinib concentrations trended downward throughout the trial (0.74 \text{ ng/mL} on Day 8 \to 0.32 \text{ ng/mL} on Day 29 \to 0.07 \text{ ng/mL} on Day 113). After 252 days of continuous delgocitinib, her leukocyte levels were 3.9 \times 10^9, lymphocyte levels were 0.6 \times 10^9/L, and neutrophil levels were 2.7 \times 10^9/L. Laboratory analyses of other hematology parameters did not indicate pancytopenia suggesting broad bone marrow suppression.
L975: The clinical reviewer concludes that a causal association between delgocitinib and the leukopenia cannot be excluded. The causality assessment is confounded by the history of leukopenia and the development of an AE of influenza 4 days prior to the reported AE of
L976: leukopenia with associated decrease in lymphocytes and neutrophils. The systemic concentration of delgocitinib trended downward during the 16-week treatment period. However, the AE of leukopenia occurred while on treatment, and the investigator (most familiar with the subject's presentation in the context of the clinical trial) assessed the leukopenia as possibly related. Given the known potential of JAK inhibitors to cause leukopenia, these factors suggest that a causal relationship between delgocitinib and the observed leukopenia TEAE (and associated shift) cannot be excluded.

L1005: For detailed information on individual subjects who received delgocitinib and had neutrophil shifts (including screening, baseline, worst postbaseline values, whether subjects returned to screening/baseline values, whether PK sampling was performed, and investigator-assessed causality), refer to Section 17.1 Clinical Safety: Additional Information.
L1006: Two subjects (0.3%) who received delgocitinib shifted from Grade 0 neutrophil values at baseline to Grade 3 neutrophil values at worst postbaseline, versus no such shift observed in subjects who received vehicle. Brief narrative summaries are below:
L1007: - A 52-year-old female (Subject [REDACTED](b)(6)) with a history of a mild nonserious COVID-19 infection 16 days prior to baseline developed neutropenia on Day 15. At screening, the subject had neutrophils of 2.1 \times 10^9/L and leukocytes of 4.2 \times 10^9; at baseline the subject had neutrophils of 2 \times 10^9/L and leukocytes of 3.4 \times 10^9/L (&lt;lower limit of normal). On Day 15, neutrophil counts decreased to 0.8 \times 10^9/L (Grade 3), and by the next visit (Day 27) had increased to 2.2 \times 10^9/L. On Day 113, neutrophils were 1.5 \times 10^9/L, and leukocytes
L1008: were 3.3 \times 10^9/L. No PK sampling was performed. When the subject entered Trial DELTA 3, she received continuous delgocitinib through Day 266 and her neutrophil levels remained stable at 1.51 \times 10^9/L. The final recorded neutrophil level was 1.6 \times 10^9/L on Day 225 (Grade 1, less than baseline of 2.1 \times 10^9/L in parent Trial DELTA 1). The final leukocyte count was 3.7 \times 10^9/L on Day 225 (stable from baseline of 3.4 \times 10^9/L in parent Trial DELTA 1).
L1009: The subject did not have any concurrent adverse events, and there were no concurrent shifts in any other hematology parameters either in trial DELTA 1 or with continuous treatment through Day 266 of trial DELTA 3 to suggest broad bone marrow suppression.
L1010: The clinical reviewer assessed that a causal association between delgocitinib and the neutrophil shift cannot be excluded based on several factors. The Grade 3 neutrophil decrease occurred during treatment on Day 15, establishing a temporal relationship. Although there was a partial recovery by Day 27, neutrophil levels remained consistently below baseline throughout both trials and did not recover to baseline values at end of 255 days of continuous treatment, suggesting a persistent effect. While the pre-existing low neutrophil count and mild nonserious COVID-19 infection during the screening period introduce some uncertainty, these factors do not fully explain the observed pattern. The lack of PK data prevents a definitive assessment of a dose-response relationship. Given the known association of JAK inhibitors with neutropenia, the preponderance of evidence suggests that a causal relationship between delgocitinib and the observed neutrophil shift cannot be excluded.
L1011: - A 43-year-old female (Subject [b] (6)) with a history of COPD, pulmonary tuberculosis (TB), varicose veins, and prior smoker developed neutropenia on Day 30. The subject had baseline neutrophil levels of 2.6 \times 10^9/L and shifted to Grade 1 (1.5 \times 10^9) on Day 30. On Day 58, the subject developed an upper respiratory tract infection and then on Day 84 neutrophils shifted to Grade 3 (0.9 \times 10^9). Plasma delgocitinib concentrations trended downward throughout the trial [0.184 \text{ ng/mL} on Day 7 \to 0.168 \text{ ng/mL} on Day 30 \to 0.05 \text{ ng/mL} on Day 112]. Neutrophil levels normalized to 2.9 \times 10^9/L by Week 16. The subject received near-continuous delgocitinib (with a 16-day off-treatment period) until Day 372 in the long-term safety trial. Baseline level neutrophils at Week 16 were 2.9 \times 10^9/L. Following an AE of mild gastroenteritis on Day 209, her neutrophils decreased to 1.1 \times 10^9/L (Grade 2) on Day 225. By Day 372, the subject had neutrophils of 2.4 \times 10^9/L (Grade 0). Leukocytes at the beginning of Trial DELTA 2 were 4.03 \times 10^9/L and at the end of Trial DELTA 3 were 4.5 \times 10^9/L. There were no shifts in any other hematology parameters either in Trial DELTA 2 or with near-continuous treatment through Day 372 of trial DELTA 3 to suggest broad bone marrow suppression.
L1012: The clinical reviewer assessed that the Grade 3 shift was unlikely related to delgocitinib based on several considerations. Firstly, there is a lack of consistent correlation between drug exposure and neutrophil levels. Plasma delgocitinib levels were trending downward at the time that the subject experienced a Grade 3 neutrophil decrease on Day 84, with the lowest recorded delgocitinib exposure on Day 112. Additionally, neutrophil levels normalized to 2.9 \times 10^9/L by Week 16, despite continued delgocitinib treatment. During the long-term safety trial while receiving near continuous delgocitinib treatment for 372 days, neutrophil levels remained mostly stable, with only a transient Grade 2 decrease on Day 225. This decrease in neutrophil level coincided with an adverse event of mild gastroenteritis, suggesting a potential alternative cause. Importantly, neutrophil levels normalized to 2.4 \times 10^9/L by the end
L1013: of Trial DELTA 3, despite prolonged delgocitinib exposure. The absence of shifts in other hematology parameters in both trials argues against broad bone marrow suppression. Furthermore, the subject's history of COPD, pulmonary TB, and smoking could contribute to fluctuations in neutrophil counts. The temporal relationship between adverse events (upper respiratory tract infection and gastroenteritis) and neutrophil decreases also suggests these infections may have played a role in the observed neutrophil shifts. Collectively, these factors suggest the neutrophil shifts are more likely attributable to other factors, making a causal relationship to delgocitinib unlikely.

L1040: ## Adverse Events for Subjects Exposed for 16 Weeks
L1041: In the primary safety pool to Week 16, AEs of “lymphocyte count decreased” were reported in 0.3% (two subjects, EAIR 0.9) of subjects who received delgocitinib, and no subjects who received vehicle; AEs “lymphopenia” were reported in 0.3% (two subjects, EAIR 0.9) of
L1042: subjects who received delgocitinib and no subjects who received vehicle. All four TEAEs were nonserious and mild in severity, none led to interruption or discontinuation of treatment, and none were considered related by the investigator. Of note, laboratory data showed that lymphocyte levels for the four subjects were all within normal range (&gt;1.0\times 10^9 cells/L) throughout the trial.
L1043: Narratives for the subjects are below:
L1044: - A 64-year-old female (b) (6) with a history of hyperlipidemia, hypertension, and polyneuritis developed lymphopenia on Day 113 after initiation of chemotherapy for gallbladder adenocarcinoma. The subject had baseline lymphocyte levels of 2.3\times 10^9 cells/L. PK sampling was not performed. On Day 30, the subject received the diagnosis of gallbladder adenocarcinoma and on Day 60 initiated chemotherapy with cisplatin and oral glucocorticoids (prednisolone). Lymphocyte levels then began to trend downward: Day 27: 2\times 10^9 cells/L → Day 72: 1.7\times 10^9 cells/L → Day 86: 1.4\times 10^9 cells/L → Day 113: 1.1\times 10^9 cells/L (when the AE “lymphocyte count decreased” was reported, considered unrelated by the investigator). Platelets and hemoglobin also trended downward during this period. After Week 16, the subject received intermittent delgocitinib until Day 366. Lymphocyte levels fluctuated throughout the trial: on Day 57 they were 2.8\times 10^9 cells/L and on Day 142 they were 1.2\times 10^9 cells/L (when the AE “lymphocyte count decreased” was reported again). At the end of the trial, lymphocytes were 1.6\times 10^9 cells/L. There was no clear pattern of changes in other hematologic parameters while she was on/off treatment with delgocitinib in Trial DELTA 3.
L1045: Given the development of adenocarcinoma with concomitant treatment with chemotherapy, the temporal association of lymphocyte changes with the initiation of chemotherapy, and the lack of a clear pattern of changes in hematologic parameters with intermittent treatment with delgocitinib in the long-term safety trial to Day 266 (i.e., under challenge/dechallenge type conditions), the clinical reviewer considered it unlikely that the AEs of “decreased lymphocyte count” were related to delgocitinib.
L1046: - A 48-year-old female (b) (6) with a history of smoking developed lymphopenia on Day 114 of delgocitinib. The subject had a screening lymphocyte count of 1.7\times 10^9 cells/L and baseline lymphocyte count of 2\times 10^9 cells/L. The lymphocyte count on the first day of the long-term safety trial was 1.4\times 10^9 cells/L. PK sampling was not performed. In Trial DELTA 3, the subject received continuous treatment with delgocitinib. The AE of “lymphocyte count decreased” was considered unrelated by investigator. At the end of Trial DELTA 3, lymphocytes were 1.5\times 10^9 cells/L (higher than her baseline in Trial DELTA 1). Laboratory analyses of other hematology parameters did not indicate pancytopenia suggestive of broad bone marrow suppression in either trial DELTA 2 or with continuous use in Trial DELTA 3. The AE is considered unlikely related to delgocitinib by the clinical reviewer.
- A 54-year-old female (b) (6) with a history of active smoking had lymphopenia on Day 1 of the trial. The subject had a screening lymphocyte count of 1.3\times 10^9 cells/L and a baseline lymphocyte count of 1.1\times 10^9 cells. An AE of lymphopenia was documented from Day 1. Counts fluctuated throughout the trial and at the end of the trial were 1.1\times 10^9 cells/L. PK sampling was not performed. The subject received continuous delgocitinib until Day 251. The lymphocyte count at the end of the trial was 1.2\times 10^9 cells/L. Laboratory analyses of
L1047: other hematology parameters did not indicate pancytopenia suggestive of broad bone marrow suppression in either trial DELTA 1 nor with continuous use in Trial DELTA 3. The AE is considered unlikely related to delgocitinib by the clinical reviewer.

L1058: ## **Summary of Hematologic Findings and Recommendations for Labeling**
L1059: The review team conducted a comprehensive analysis of hematologic parameters for subjects in the vehicle-controlled 16-week period and in subjects who received continuous exposure to
L1060: delgocitinib for 52 weeks. This included analysis of shifts from baseline to worst postbaseline values, analyses of individual subject narratives, analysis of correlation between hematologic abnormalities and plasma concentrations when collected, cytopenia TEAEs, and detailed subject-level analyses of hematologic lab values at screening/baseline, throughout the trial, and at end of trial (refer to Section 17.1 Clinical Safety: Additional Information).
L1061: There were no significant imbalances between treatment groups for hemoglobin levels. No subjects developed Grade 3 decreases and only a few subjects developed Grade 2 shifts. Thrombocyte shifts were similar between delgocitinib and vehicle groups. There were isolated cases of Grade 2 or 3 decreases that may have been impacted by other etiologic factors described in their medical histories.
L1062: There was a slight imbalance in neutrophil levels between the delgocitinib and vehicle groups: two subjects (0.3%) on delgocitinib shifting from Grade 0 to Grade 3 (versus none in vehicle), for which causality related to delgocitinib could not be excluded in one subject (refer to narrative for Subject (b)(6)). Lymphocyte findings showed one transient shift from Grade 2 to Grade 3 in the delgocitinib group, and similar proportions of Grade 0 to Grade 2 shifts between treatment groups. For leukocyte counts, though no subjects in either group had shifts to Grade 3, a greater proportion of subjects who received delgocitinib (13.3%) developed a shift from Grade 0 to Grade 1 than subjects who received vehicle (6.1%). One subject developed a Grade 1 to 2 shift with an associated TEAE of leukopenia, which was considered possibly related by investigator and for which causality related to delgocitinib could not be excluded by the clinical reviewer (refer to narrative for Subject (b)(6)).
L1063: In the analysis of overall cytopenias, the review team observed a notable numerical imbalance in subjects experiencing any worsening grade shift in leukocytes: 13.6% (EAIR 44.2) in the delgocitinib group versus 6.2% (EAIR 21.3) in the vehicle group during the 16-week vehicle-controlled period (refer to Table 53). The leukocyte imbalance was primarily driven by shifts from Grade 0 at baseline to Grade 1 postbaseline. Though the clinical significance of these shifts is unclear, since none resulted in drug discontinuation or serious adverse events, there is biologic plausibility the shifts may have been related to JAK inhibition due to known class effects of JAK inhibitors with cytopenias. Smaller imbalances were observed for neutrophils (4.4%, EAIR 14.2 for delgocitinib versus 3.7%, EAIR 12.8 for vehicle) and lymphocytes (2.5%, EAIR 8.1 for delgocitinib versus 1.9%, EAIR 6.4 for vehicle).
L1064: Given the totality of these findings – the leukocyte imbalance in the vehicle-controlled period and select cases where a causal relationship with delgocitinib could not be excluded, measurable systemic exposure (refer to Section 5.2 of this review), and the known association of JAK inhibitors with cytopenias with biologic plausibility of association – the review team recommends including leukopenia and neutropenia as adverse reactions occurring in &lt;1% of subjects in Section 6 of labeling. Since all Grade 3 shifts across all hematologic parameters observed with 52 weeks of continuous treatment were isolated findings, improved despite continuous treatment, and had a low incidence overall, and since no hematologic shifts were associated with serious adverse events, baseline CBC and intermittent hematologic monitoring is not recommended.
L1065: ## 7.6.1.7. Assessment of Drug-Induced Liver Injury, Pooled Analyses, Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2
L1066: There did not appear to be a safety concern related to hepatic toxicity for subjects who received delgocitinib. In Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2, no subjects developed drug-induced liver injury (DILI). A few subjects developed adverse events related to hepatic dysfunction in both treatment groups (&lt;1%). These TEAEs were primarily mild in severity, and none led to change in IMP. Figure 9 and Table 60 show a screening assessment for potential cases of serious DILI. No subjects had Hy's law or concurrent elevations of alanine aminotransferase (ALT)/AST \ge 3 \times upper limit of normal (ULN) with bilirubin \ge 2 \times ULN.
L1067: According to the Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot below, there were a few subjects with findings suggestive of potential cholestatic DILI and Temple's corollary (refer to Figure 9 and Table 59 below). However, the imbalances between treatment groups were small, and review of the shifts from baseline in hepatic enzymes and individual subject narratives did not support a safety signal.
L1068: Of 1,062 subjects enrolled in Trials DELTA 1, DELTA 2, and 1273, five subjects (four subjects who received delgocitinib cream and one subject who received vehicle), developed transient elevations in ALT/AST values exceeding 3 \times ULN after baseline with concurrent elevations in bilirubin (bilirubin &lt; 2 \times ULN). All five subjects had isolated aminotransferase elevations during the trial, which normalized at the subsequent assessment despite continued use of the investigational product. Three subjects in the delgocitinib treatment group developed adverse events related to liver parameters. All of these AEs were nonserious and assessed as not related to the trial drug by the investigator and clinical reviewer.

L1091: ## 7.6.1.8. Vital Signs, Pooled Analyses, Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2
L1092: Vital signs (resting blood pressure, pulse, and body temperature) were assessed according to the schedule of trial procedures in Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2. Vital signs were measured in a sitting or supine position following at least 5 minutes of rest.
L1093: Analysis of shift tables for vital signs showed no clinically relevant differences in vital sign findings between treatment groups from baseline to end of treatment. However, there was an imbalance in adverse event reporting related to hypertension, which was not considered to be causally related to systemic exposure to delgocitinib by the review team, as discussed in detail below.
L1094: ## **Hypertension**
L1095: In Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2, there was a numerical difference in the reporting of AEs related to vital signs driven by the PT of “hypertension”: seven events in seven subjects in the delgocitinib group (1.1%) versus no subjects in the vehicle group. There were no increases in the rate of hypertension over time in subjects continuously treated with delgocitinib for 52 weeks (see the spaghetti plots below). Subjects who were randomized to delgocitinib had slightly higher baseline medical history of hypertension (17.4% of subjects on delgocitinib versus 14.3% of subjects on vehicle), but were otherwise comparable in terms of age, gender, body mass index (BMI), and history of diabetes mellitus. All of the subjects with reported AE of hypertension had elevated systolic blood pressure levels at screening and/or baseline.
L1096: None of the AEs of hypertension were serious or considered related to IMP by investigator or led to discontinuation of IMP or withdrawal from trial. Analysis of individual subject profiles did not suggest a time to onset of hypertension relative to the duration of treatment. (see Figure 10 and Figure 11 below).

In the pooled Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2, 11 subjects (1.7%) who received delgocitinib had systolic blood pressure above the Grade 3 threshold of \ge 160 mm Hg. Of these subjects, four subjects shifted from a Grade 1 abnormal value at baseline, four subjects shifted from Grade 2 abnormal value at baseline, and three subjects already had a Grade 3 abnormal value at baseline. L1135: Finally, in cardiac safety Trial LP0133-1409 there were no significant changes in blood pressure observed after oral dosing.
L1136: Given the totality of evidence presented above, the observed numerical difference in rates of hypertension between delgocitinib and vehicle appears to be a chance finding and is not considered to be causally related to systemic exposure to delgocitinib. The reported adverse events of hypertension may instead reflect the imbalance of subjects with a history of hypertension at baseline between treatment groups and elevations in blood pressure at baseline rather than a causal association with delgocitinib cream.
L1137: ## 7.6.1.9. Subgroups, Pooled Analyses, Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2
L1138: There are no ADRs that occurred in ≥1% in the delgocitinib group and greater than vehicle proposed for labeling (refer to Section 7.6.1.5); therefore, analysis of common ADRs by common demographic subgroups was not performed.
L1139: Since there were no common ADRs ≥1% proposed for labeling, the review team conducted analyses on adverse events identified during the placebo-controlled periods of Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 for the following subgroups: sex at birth (male, female), age (18 to 64, 65 to 84, ≥65, ≥75, ≥85 years of age), race (White, Black or African American, Asian, American Indian or Alaska Native, Native Hawaiian or other Pacific Islander, Multiple, and Other), and ethnicity (Hispanic or Latino, not Hispanic or Latino). There did not appear to be clinically meaningful differences in the frequency of TEAEs by demographic subgroup after accounting for the individual subgroup sample size, as summarized below in Table 66.

L1149: ## 7.6.1.10. Exposure-Adjusted Pooled Analyses, Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2
L1150: Exposure-adjusted incidence rate analysis for pooled trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 is presented below in Table 68. L1156: ## 7.6.2. Safety Results, Trial 1273
L1157: The Applicant submitted additional safety data from a randomized (1:1:1:1), vehicle-controlled, double-blind, dose-ranging Trial LP0133-1273, that evaluated the efficacy and safety of multiple concentrations [1 mg/g (n=52), 3 mg/g (n=51), 8 mg/g (n=52), and 20 mg/g (n=53)] of delgocitinib cream compared with vehicle n=50) in the treatment of 258 subjects with mild to severe CHE.
L1158: NDA 219155
L1159: ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1160: Key differences in the study population and study design between Trial 1273 and Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2:
L1161: - Trial 1273 enrolled a broader study population. Trial 1273 evaluated subjects with mild to severe CHE, whereas Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 evaluated subjects with moderate to severe CHE. Of the 24 subjects with mild CHE enrolled in the relevant groups of Trial 1273, 12 of 53 subjects received delgocitinib cream, 2%, and 12 of 50 subjects received vehicle.
- Trial 1273 utilized a different IGA scale than in Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2.
- Trial 1273 evaluated a different formulation than Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2, specifically containing a reduced concentration of the [REDACTED] (b) (4) from [REDACTED] (b) (4) to [REDACTED] (b) (4).
L1162: The review team analyzed safety data from Trial 1273 both individually and pooled with data from Trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 and identified no new safety signals (see Table 69 below).

L1175: ## 7.6.3. Safety Results, Trial DELTA FORCE
L1176: ## 7.6.3.1. Overview of Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events
## Summary, Trial DELTA FORCE
L1177: Alitretinoin (marketed in several European countries, Canada, Israel, and South Korea as Tocitino) was first approved in 2008 for the treatment of severe CHE in adults who are unresponsive to treatment with potent topical corticosteroids. The Applicant conducted Trial DELTA FORCE outside the United States in adult subjects ≥18 years of age with severe CHE.
L1178: ## Design and Study Population
L1179: Trial DELTA FORCE was a phase 3, randomized, assessor-blinded, active-controlled, parallel-group, 2-arm trial that compared the efficacy and safety of delgocitinib cream, 2% twice daily with alitretinoin capsules 30 mg (or 10 mg) once daily. Approximately 500 adult subjects with severe CHE who had a documented recent history of inadequate response to treatment with TCS or for whom TCS was medically inadvisable were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to receive delgocitinib cream twice daily or alitretinoin capsules 30 mg once daily for up to 24 weeks (refer to Figure 12 below). Randomization was stratified by subtype (hyperkeratotic/nonhyperkeratotic) and region (North America/Europe).
L1180: The primary endpoint was change in HECSI score from baseline to Week 12.
L1181: NDA 219155
L1182: ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1183: Safety monitoring included physical examinations, AEs, clinical laboratory data, vital signs, pregnancy testing, and ECGs.
L1184: Figure 12. Schematic of Trial DELTA FORCE
L1185: Source: Clinical Study Report
L1186: Abbreviations: HECISI, Hand Eczema Severity Index; IGA-CHE, Investigator's Global Assessment for chronic hand eczema
L1187: The key design features are tabulated below in Table 71.

L1192: ## Results
L1193: A total of 513 adult subjects with severe CHE received delgocitinib cream or alitretinoin for up to 24 weeks. Results for delgocitinib cream did not identify any new safety concerns. The safety analysis for delgocitinib cream compared to alitretinoin demonstrated fewer AEs, SAEs, severe AEs, related AEs, and AEs leading to investigational product discontinuation.
L1194: These results are summarized in Table 72 through Table 76 below.

L1219: Risk difference (with 95% confidence interval) is shown between total treatment and comparator.

L1244: ## 7.6.3.2. Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events, Trial DELTA FORCE
L1245: The safety profile of delgocitinib cream in Trial DELTA FORCE was similar to the safety profile observed in phase 3 trials DELTA 1, DELTA 2, and DELTA 3. No new safety signals were identified. Overall, subjects who received delgocitinib developed fewer TEAEs compared to subjects who received alitretinoin in Trial DELTA FORCE, as illustrated in Table 77, Table 78, and Table 79 below.

L1283: ## 7.7. Key Safety Review Issues
L1284: Refer to list from Section 3.1.
L1285: ## 7.7.1. Impact of the Formulation Change on the Assessment of Pharmacokinetics and Safety
L1286: The Applicant evaluated the final to-be-marketed formulation in most of the trials in the clinical development program, including maximal use studies (LP0133-2285 and DELTA 2). However,
L1287: the Applicant also used an early formulation in the development program. The difference between the final formulation and the early formulation is the amount of [REDACTED] (b) (4), which was [REDACTED] (b) (4) mg/g in the final formulation compared to [REDACTED] (b) (4) mg/g in the early formulation.
L1288: Per the Drug Product review, [REDACTED] (b) (4) is used as [REDACTED] (b) (4). Based on the submitted stability data, the formulation with the reduced [REDACTED] (b) (4) met the desired stability criteria [REDACTED] (b) (4), which indicated that the reduced level of [REDACTED] (b) (4) is sufficient [REDACTED] (b) (4). The change in the formulation did not warrant any PK or clinical bridging study as determined by Biopharmaceutics review. Furthermore, Biopharmaceutics determined the change in the formulation to be minor and the bridge established based on chemistry, manufacturing, and controls (CMC) information submitted.
L1289: Refer to the Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) drug product review for the detailed information on the assessment of formulation change and supporting data.
L1290: ## 7.7.2. Impact of the Lack of Run-In Period on the Assessment of Safety
L1291: The proposed inclusion criteria for the phase 3 trials (DELTA 1, DELTA 2, and DELTA 3) specified enrollment of subjects with “a recent history of inadequate response to treatment with TCS within 1 year prior to screening, or for whom TCS therapy was medically inadvisable (e.g., due to safety concerns or side effects).”
L1292: At the End-of-Phase 2 meeting, the Agency advised that an inadequate response to TCS therapy may not be reliably determined based on medical history alone. To address this, the Agency recommended incorporating a run-in period with standard care measures (e.g., moisturizers, irritant avoidance) and TCS therapy to prospectively confirm inadequate response.
L1293: The Applicant, however, did not incorporate a run-in-period and expressed concern that such a run-in period would unnecessarily re-expose subjects to therapies previously deemed ineffective and delay access to an investigational product in a setting with limited treatment options. In their NDA submission, the Applicant further argued that defining inadequate response based on a single short course of TCS with limited follow-up does not appropriately reflect the chronic, relapsing nature of CHE. The Applicant noted that while patients may experience temporary improvement with a short course of potent TCS, the durability of response, timing of relapse, need for retreatment, and cumulative treatment burden remain uncertain. Therefore, the Applicant concluded that selection based on documented medical history was more reflective of the target patient population.
L1294: To support their decision to omit a run-in period from the study design and to confirm that their enrollment criteria adequately defined a study population with “inadequate response to treatment with topical corticosteroids,” the Applicant conducted post hoc analyses of the safety and efficacy data from their phase 3 trials. Their analyses compared data from subjects who discontinued daily TCS no earlier than 2, 4, or 8 weeks prior to screening, with subjects who used daily TCS on the hands after screening, up to 2 weeks before baseline (“run-in-like” exposure). The Applicant conducted post hoc subgroup analyses of the primary and key secondary endpoints at Week 16 by these TCS run-in-like periods. Based on AE distribution by SOC and PT, the Applicant concluded that the incidence of AEs was comparable between the
L1295: delgocitinib and vehicle groups, with no clinically meaningful differences observed between subjects with or without TCS run-in-like exposure. The data presented in the review team's Table 80 and Table 81 below suggest that the impact of the lack of a formal run-in period on the assessment of safety appears low, with no meaningful differences observed in SAEs or TEAEs between subjects with and without run-in-like periods. Therefore, the review team's analyses confirmed the Applicant's conclusions.
