L1320: ## 7.7.3. Evaluation of JAK Inhibitor Class Safety Labeling for Inflammatory Conditions in Context of the Delgocitinib Development Program
L1321: ## Infection
L1322: Based on the safety considerations associated with the class of JAK inhibitors and the existing Boxed Warning for serious infections in JAK inhibitor labeling for inflammatory conditions, the Applicant identified infection as a safety review focus area.
L1323: Serious and opportunistic infections, including tuberculosis, have been reported in patients receiving JAK inhibitors, including both topical (e.g., ruxolitinib) and systemic agents (e.g., abrocitinib, baricitinib, tofacitinib). An increased risk of herpes zoster and other serious infections associated with JAK inhibitor therapy is hypothesized to be related, in part, to the suppression of NK cell function. Natural killer cells play a key role in antiviral immunity, particularly in controlling varicella zoster virus reactivation (Meudec et al. 2023; Winthrop et al. 2023).
L1324: The following subjects were excluded in the phase 3 clinical trials for delgocitinib: those with clinically significant infection (e.g., “impetiginized hand eczema”) on the hands, systemic infection or serious skin infection requiring systemic treatment within 28 days prior to baseline, and those with history of any known primary immunodeficiency disorder, including a positive HIV test at screening.
L1325: ## **Serious Infections**
L1326: No serious infections at the application site were observed in Trials DELTA 1, DELTA 2, DELTA 3, and 1273 in subjects who received delgocitinib. The following serious infections were reported in Trials DELTA 1, DELTA 2, DELTA 3, and 1273 in subjects who received delgocitinib: peritonsillar abscess (2 cases), tonsillitis, chronic sinusitis, bacterial keratitis, paranasal sinus abscess, chest infection, pneumonia, right maxillary sinusitis due to aspergillosis, periappendicular abscess, gastroenteritis, urinary tract infection, bilateral interstitial COVID-19 pneumonia, appendicitis, severe pyelonephritis, pseudomembranous colitis, and streptococcal infection. Most of the serious infections were single events (except when noted). There was no clustering of events. Opportunistic infections included vulvovaginal mycotic infection, fungal genital infection, herpes simplex (27 cases), herpes zoster (3 cases), and eczema herpeticum (1 case). There were no reported cases of tuberculosis. There were no reported imbalances in serious infections between subjects who received delgocitinib versus vehicle.
L1327: In open-label extension Trial DELTA 3, there were cases of eczema herpeticum (one subject delgocitinib, zero vehicle) and herpes zoster (two subjects delgocitinib, zero vehicle). Narratives for these subjects are presented below.
L1328: ## **Eczema Herpeticum**
L1329: A 34-year-old female ( (b) (6) ) with a history of atopic dermatitis, asthma, contact dermatitis, allergic rhinitis, and hypertension developed eczema herpeticum affecting the right side of the neck and bilateral eyelids on Day 144 of near-continuous treatment with delgocitinib. She received antiviral therapy, with resolution of EH in 20 days. The investigator
L1330: considered the event possibly related to the investigational product. No PK samples were obtained. Neutrophil levels [10^9/L] at baseline of Trial DELTA 1 → end of DELTA 1 → and end of Trial DELTA 3 were 4.1 → 4.3 → 3.3 (i.e., lower than baseline).
L1331: Given the timing of onset during active treatment and the known pathophysiology of eczema herpeticum, a causal relationship with delgocitinib cannot be excluded. Janus kinase inhibitors may impair antiviral immune responses by disrupting cytokine signaling critical for the function of NK cells. Natural killer cells play a central role in early host defense against HSV, and their suppression may increase susceptibility to disseminated HSV infection (i.e., eczema herpeticum), particularly in patients with atopic dermatitis and other underlying skin barrier defects. This mechanistic plausibility, in combination with the temporal association and downward trend of neutrophils over time, supports the conclusion that the investigational product may have contributed to the event (Traidl et al. 2021; Wang et al. 2021).
L1332: ## Herpes Zoster
L1333: - A 42-year-old male (b)(6) with a history of diabetes, hyperlipidemia, hypertension, and hyperthyroidism developed moderate HZ on the face adjacent to the lips on Day 252 of continuous treatment with delgocitinib. The narrative did not include family history or other risk factors for HZ. The subject received antiviral therapy. Levels for hematologic parameters (leukocytes, lymphocytes, and neutrophils) remained stable from baseline to the end of treatment:
L1334: - Leukocytes [10^9/L]: 11 → 10 → 12
- Lymphocytes [10^9/L]: 2.7 → 2.2 → 2.4
- Neutrophils [10^9/L]: 7.4 → 6.9 → 8.4
L1335: The investigator assessed the AE as not related to the investigational product. Given the subject's age &lt;50 years of age (with advanced age a risk factor for HZ) and the timing of onset during active treatment, a causal role for delgocitinib cannot be excluded.
L1336: - A 49-year-old female (b)(6) with a history of atopic dermatitis developed mild herpes zoster on the back on Day 121 of delgocitinib. Family history was unknown, and the subject had no documented risk factors for HZ. The subject received antiviral therapy and continued delgocitinib uninterrupted. Levels for hematologic parameters (leukocytes, lymphocytes, and neutrophils) remained stable from baseline to the end of treatment:
L1337: - Leukocytes [10^9/L]: 5.9 → 4.9 → 6.5
- Lymphocytes [10^9/L]: 1.9 → 1.5 → 2.1
- Neutrophils [10^9/L]: 3.3 → 2.7 → 3.5
L1338: PK levels of delgocitinib remained stable in Trial DELTA 2 (0.26 ng/ml on Day 8 → 0.19 ng/ml on Day 29 → 0.16 ng/mL on Day 113). The investigator assessed the AE as not related to the investigational product. Patients with AD have a greater risk of developing HZ. However, given the absence of traditional HZ risk factors and the timing of onset during active treatment, a causal role for delgocitinib cannot be excluded.
L1339: ## Tuberculosis and Nontuberculosis Atypical Mycobacterial Infection
L1340: The Boxed Warning in JAK inhibitor labeling for inflammatory conditions includes a warning for increased risk of TB and provides specific instructions to test for latent TB before and during
L1341: therapy and to treat latent TB prior to use. The warning also instructs clinicians to monitor all patients for active TB during treatment, even patients with initial negative latent TB tests.
L1342: TB was not assessed at baseline nor actively in the phase 3 trials for delgocitinib. No cases of tuberculosis or nontuberculosis atypical mycobacterial infection were reported in the phase 3 trials. While no TB cases were observed in the delgocitinib clinical trials, the absence of systematic TB screening and monitoring in these trials limits the ability to fully characterize the TB risk profile.
L1343: Cases of EH and HZ were observed and will be included in Warning and Precaution section of the product labeling. In addition, although cases of TB were not observed in the pivotal clinical trials, labeling will include potential risks for serious infection will be included in the Warnings and Precautions section of labeling.
L1344: ## **Mortality**
L1345: Class labeling for JAK inhibitors indicated for the treatment of inflammatory conditions includes a boxed warning for all-cause mortality. In a large, randomized, postmarketing safety study of another oral JAK inhibitor in subjects with rheumatoid arthritis ages 50 years of age and older with at least one cardiovascular risk factor, a higher rate of all-cause mortality, including sudden cardiovascular death, was observed in subjects treated with the JAK inhibitor compared with subjects treated with TNF blockers.
L1346: In the development program for CHE, there were three deaths among subjects who received delgocitinib and none among subjects who received only vehicle. Investigators assessed all three deaths as not related to delgocitinib (refer to Section 7.6.1.2 of this review). Two cases did not have a persuasive temporal association with the drug product and one case lacked sufficient information to support any conclusion. Overall, delgocitinib did not appear to increase the risk of all-cause mortality compared to vehicle.
L1347: Although higher rates of all-cause mortality were not observed in the pivotal clinical trials, potential risks related to JAK inhibition will be included in the Warnings and Precautions section of labeling.
L1348: ## **Malignancy and Lymphoproliferative Disorders**
L1349: Class labeling for JAK inhibitors indicated for the treatment of inflammatory conditions includes a boxed warning for lymphoma and other malignancies. The literature supports a key role for the JAK-STAT signaling pathway in the development of cancer cells and their proliferation, differentiation, and survival (Harrison 2012). In a large, randomized, postmarketing safety study of a systemic JAK inhibitor in subjects with rheumatoid arthritis, a higher rate of malignancies (including lymphomas and lung cancer) was observed in subjects treated with the JAK inhibitor compared to those treated with TNF blockers.
L1350: There were no reports of lymphomas or lung cancers in the development program for delgocitinib. However, there are reports of the development of nonmelanoma skin cancers in trials for both topical and systemic JAK inhibitors.
L1351: There were no imbalances between treatment groups in the development of malignancies in Trials DELTA 1, DELTA 2, and DELTA 3. The following malignancies were reported in subjects who received delgocitinib: gallbladder adenocarcinoma (one in delgocitinib, zero in
L1352: NDA 219155
L1353: ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1354: vehicle), metastatic esophageal cancer (one in delgocitinib, zero in vehicle), basal cell carcinoma (one in delgocitinib, zero in vehicle), and intraductal proliferative breast lesion (one in delgocitinib, zero in vehicle).
L1355: Although malignancy and lymphoproliferative adverse reactions were not observed in the pivotal clinical trials, potential risks related to JAK inhibition will be included in the Warnings and Precautions section of labeling.
L1356: ## Basal Cell Carcinoma
L1357: A 77-year-old male with a history of chronic sun exposure developed a nodular basal cell carcinoma on the left ear on Day 57 of delgocitinib. Biopsy on Day 92 confirmed nodular basal cell carcinoma, and the lesion was surgically excised on Day 113. The event was classified as serious. Delgocitinib treatment continued without interruption, and the event was considered resolved. The investigator did not attribute the event to delgocitinib. Based on subject risk factors, lesion location outside of treatment area, and timing, this reviewer considers it unlikely that the AE was related to the investigational product.
L1358: ## Gallbladder Cancer
L1359: A 64-year-old female with no relevant medical history was diagnosed with adenocarcinoma of the gallbladder on Day 29 of treatment with delgocitinib. The subject presented with “colic pain” on Day 1, and an escalation of symptoms (not defined) on Day 20. Biliary magnetic resonance imaging on Day 27 revealed 2 gallstones and cholecystectomy was performed on the following day. Pathology showed gallbladder adenocarcinoma. Delgocitinib was continued without interruption. On Day 49, subject received chemotherapy. The investigator assessed the SAE as not related. In view of the long latency period for the development of gallbladder cancer and diagnosis early in the course of the trial, the review team agrees that the SAE of gallbladder cancer is likely unrelated to delgocitinib.
L1360: ## Metastatic Esophageal Cancer
L1361: Metastatic esophageal cancer from Trial DELTA 2: see narrative under death.
L1362: ## Breast Cancer
L1363: A 55-year-old female with a history of basal cell carcinoma, obesity (BMI 46.7), and two prior pregnancies (with two children but no breastfeeding) developed ductal carcinoma in situ on Day 286. The subject had a routine mammogram with ultrasound on Day 174 that showed calcifications in upper right breast. The subject was diagnosed with right breast ductal carcinoma in situ (DCIS). Pathology report from the lumpectomy showed no residual DCIS. The subject completed delgocitinib without interruption. The subject's family history for breast cancer was unknown. The investigator concluded the event was unlikely to be related to delgocitinib. Because the subject developed breast cancer ~9.5 months after first dose of delgocitinib and had several known risk factors (female, obesity, history of other cancer, i.e., basal cell carcinoma), the review team concludes that it is less likely that this AE was related to delgocitinib. DCIS tends to be slow-growing and takes many years to develop (some studies suggest anywhere between 3 to 9 years).
L1364: In the Exposure pool (Trials DELTA 1, DELTA 2, DELTA 3, 1273, DELTA FORCE, 2285, 1275, 1181), cases of malignancy also included a malignant melanoma that developed in a subject with AD who received vehicle (Trial 1275). The 120-day Safety Report included two additional reports of malignancy in subjects who received delgocitinib, including pancreatic carcinoma and papillary thyroid cancer, considered unrelated by the review team.
L1365: ## **Major Adverse Cardiovascular Events**
L1366: Class labeling for JAK inhibitors indicated for the treatment of chronic inflammatory conditions includes a Boxed Warning for MACE, defined as cardiovascular death, myocardial infarction, and stroke. In a large, randomized, postmarketing safety study of another JAK inhibitor in subjects with rheumatoid arthritis who were 50 years of age and older with at least one cardiovascular risk factor, a higher rate of MACE was observed with the JAK inhibitor compared to those treated with TNF blockers. Subjects who were current or past smokers were at additional increased risk.
L1367: Baseline risk factors for MACE (cardiovascular death, nonfatal myocardial infarction, nonfatal stroke) were well balanced between the treatment arms of trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2:
L1368: - Male: 36.4% delgocitinib versus 33.9% vehicle
- Mean BMI: 27 delgocitinib versus 27.6 vehicle
- Medical histories of:Diabetes mellitus: 4.2% delgocitinib versus 5.6% vehicle
- Hypertension: 17.4% delgocitinib versus 14.3% vehicle
- Myocardial infarction: 0.6% delgocitinib versus none vehicle
- Stroke: 0.2% delgocitinib versus none vehicle
- Thrombosis: 0.2% delgocitinib versus 1.2% vehicle
L1369: In the phase 3 trials for the evaluation of delgocitinib for the treatment of CHE, a 71-year-old male subject developed a serious adverse event of MI and died 2 weeks later. This SAE occurred approximately 7 months after the last dose of delgocitinib. The subject received antihypertensive medication for 3 months prior to the event. Given the timing of the event and risk factors (age &gt;50 years, male gender, and history of hypertension) for MACE, this event was unlikely related to delgocitinib, though causal association cannot be ruled out (refer to Section 7.6.1.2 of this review).
L1370: No clinically relevant differences in vital sign assessments (blood pressure and pulse) or ECGs were observed during the treatment period in either the delgocitinib or vehicle group in the phase 3 trials.
L1371: Finally, cardiac safety was supported by Trial LP0133-1409, a phase 1, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group, single-dose clinical trial in healthy subjects. An oral (capsule) formulation was used for this trial. Forty subjects received single-dose treatment, with 8 subjects in each dose group (placebo and delgocitinib 1.5 mg, 3 mg, 6 mg, and 12 mg). Delgocitinib did not have a clinically relevant effect on ECG parameters as assessed in this trial, and there were no AEs of SMQ Torsade de pointes or QT prolongation. In this trial the mean C_{max} was 96.4 ng/mL for the highest oral dose assessed (12 mg), compared to a mean C_{max} of 0.5 ng/mL anticipated therapeutic exposure for the topical cream. Therefore, the highest dose of
L1372: NDA 219155
ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1373: 12 mg after oral administration covers the maximum anticipated therapeutic exposure (C_{max}) by 193-fold, and for Section 12.2 of labeling the following language is recommended: "At 193 times the mean maximal concentration provided by the recommended topical dose, clinically significant corrected QT interval (QTc) prolongation was not observed." Refer to the review by the Cardiac-Safety Interdisciplinary Review Team dated March 3, 2025, for details.
L1374: Although cardiovascular adverse reactions are not observed in the pivotal clinical trials, potential risks related to JAK inhibition will be included in the Warnings and Precautions section of labeling.
L1375: ## **Thrombosis**
L1376: Labeling for JAK inhibitors that treat inflammatory conditions carry a Boxed Warning for thrombosis, including increased incidence of pulmonary embolism and venous and arterial thrombosis.
L1377: Deep vein thrombosis and pulmonary embolism were prespecified as AESIs in the trial protocols to capture additional information on such events. No AESIs of deep vein thrombosis, pulmonary embolism, or arterial thrombosis were reported with delgocitinib in the development program.
L1378: Superficial thrombophlebitis was reported in two subjects who received delgocitinib cream. A 43-year-old female (b)(6) with history of bilateral varicose veins developed an AE of superficial thrombophlebitis of the right thigh on Day 135. In addition, a 54-year-old female who was enrolled in Trial DELTA FORCE with history of hypertension developed superficial thrombophlebitis on Day 52. Both events were assessed as nonserious, of moderate severity, did not lead to withdrawal from trial, and were not considered related to the IMP by the review team.
L1379: One subject who received delgocitinib developed one event (IR: 0.51) of thrombocytosis in the phase 3 trials up to 16 weeks, while no subjects who received vehicle developed such events. The subject had an ongoing AE of gallbladder carcinoma and reported concomitant antineoplastic medications at the time of the AE. The event was assessed as mild and not related and did not lead to withdrawal from trial or discontinuation of the IMP. Platelet counts decreased to normal range at the next assessment. During the 0- to 52-week period of continuous use, no events of thrombocytosis were reported. There did not appear to be any meaningful increases in thrombocyte counts in subjects who received delgocitinib in the Week 0 to 16 period and in subjects who received 52 weeks of continuous delgocitinib.
L1380: Although cases of thrombosis are not observed in the pivotal clinical trials, potential risks related to JAK inhibition will be included in the Warnings and Precautions section of labeling.
L1381: ## **Gastrointestinal Perforation**
L1382: Gastrointestinal perforation is a known risk associated with treatment with other JAK inhibitors, and reflected in labeling under Warnings and Precautions for JAK inhibitors that treat certain chronic inflammatory conditions. Patients with underlying inflammatory bowel conditions, diverticulitis, or other GI disorders may be at increased risk of GI perforation. There were no reports of GI perforation in the delgocitinib development program. Additionally, review of GI-related adverse events that could potentially signal underlying perforation risk showed no cases of acute abdominal pain requiring hospitalization, peritonitis or signs of intra-abdominal
L1383: infection, bowel obstruction or ileus of unclear etiology, severe GI bleeding requiring intervention, or emergency abdominal surgical procedures to suggest that delgocitinib increases the risk of GI perforation. Therefore, GI perforation is not proposed for inclusion in labeling.
L1384: ## **Liver Enzyme Elevations**
L1385: Warnings regarding the potential for “elevated liver enzymes” are included in Warnings and Precautions, Laboratory Abnormalities, and Adverse Reactions of labeling for certain JAK inhibitors indicated for the treatment of inflammatory conditions (e.g., baricitinib, tofacitinib, upadacitinib).
L1386: In the phase 3 trials for delgocitinib, subjects with positive hepatitis B surface antigen or hepatitis C virus antibody serology at screening and screening ALT or AST level \ge 2.0 \times \text{ULN} were excluded. In the phase 3 trials there were no clinically relevant changes or differences between treatment groups in liver parameters (alkaline phosphatase, ALT, AST, bilirubin, or albumin), there were no reported adverse events suggesting hepatotoxic effects, and no subjects met Hy’s law criteria or had concurrent elevations of ALT/AST \ge 3 \times \text{ULN} with bilirubin \ge 2 \times \text{ULN}. Refer to Section 7.6.1.7 of this review.
L1387: The clinical trials and long-term safety data also did not show signs of drug-induced liver injury, hepatic failure, or any patterns suggesting hepatotoxic effects from delgocitinib cream, 2%, and “elevated liver enzymes” are not proposed for inclusion in labeling.
L1388: ## **Hematologic Abnormalities**
L1389: Erythropoietin, granulocyte colony-stimulating factor, and granulocyte-macrophage colony-stimulating factor are modulated by JAK2 signaling. Therefore, inhibition of JAK2 may result in reduced production of erythrocytes and leukocytes (Brizzi et al. 1996). Hematologic abnormalities are known risks associated with treatment with other JAK inhibitors to treat inflammatory conditions (e.g., thrombocytopenia, anemia, and neutropenia for topical ruxolitinib; thrombocytopenia and lymphopenia for abrocitinib). The clinical trials for delgocitinib did not have predefined exclusion criteria based on hematologic parameters, and hematologic parameters were monitored periodically.
L1390: The review team conducted a comprehensive analysis of hematologic parameters for subjects in the vehicle-controlled 16-week period and in subjects who received continuous exposure to delgocitinib for 52 weeks. This included analysis of shifts from baseline to worst postbaseline values (refer to Hematology above), analyses of individual subject narratives, analysis of correlation between PK samples and shifts and AEs (refer to Section 17.1 Clinical Safety: Additional Information), cytopenia TEAEs (refer to Table 44), and detailed subject-level analyses of hematologic lab values at screening/baseline, throughout the trial, and at end of trial (refer to Section 17.1 Clinical Safety: Additional Information).
L1391: No significant imbalances were observed for hemoglobin levels, with no subjects experiencing Grade 3 decreases and only a few subjects showing Grade 2 shifts, comparable between treatment groups. Thrombocyte shifts were similar between delgocitinib and vehicle groups, with isolated cases of Grade 2 or 3 decreases attributed to confounding medical histories.
L1392: Neutrophil findings showed two subjects (0.3%) on delgocitinib shifting from Grade 0 to Grade 3 (versus none in vehicle), for which causality attributable to delgocitinib could not be ruled out in one subject (refer to narrative for Subject (b)(6)). Lymphocyte findings
L1393: showed one transient shift from Grade 2 to Grade 3 in the delgocitinib group, and similar proportions of Grade 0 to Grade 2 shifts between treatment groups. For leukocyte counts, though no subjects in either arm had shifts to Grade 3, a greater proportion of subjects who received delgocitinib (13.3%) experienced a shift to Grade 1 than subjects who received vehicle (6.1%), and one subject experienced a Grade 1 to 2 shift with associated TEAE of leukopenia which was considered possibly related by investigator and for which causality to delgocitinib could not be ruled out by the clinical reviewer (refer to narrative for Subject (b) (6)).
L1394: In the analysis of overall cytopenias, the review team observed a notable numerical imbalance in subjects experiencing any worsening grade shift in leukocytes: 13.6% (EAIR 44.2) for delgocitinib versus 6.2% (EAIR 21.3) for vehicle during the 16-week vehicle-controlled period (refer to Table 53). Smaller imbalances were observed for neutrophils (4.4%, EAIR 14.2 for delgocitinib versus 3.7%, EAIR 12.8 for vehicle) and lymphocytes (2.5%, EAIR 8.1 for delgocitinib versus 1.9%, EAIR 6.4 for vehicle). The leukocyte imbalance was primarily driven by shifts from Grade 0 at baseline to Grade 1 postbaseline. Given these collective findings – the leukocyte imbalance and select cases where a causal relationship with delgocitinib could not be ruled out, non-negligible systemic exposure (refer to Section 5.2 Clinical Pharmacology of this review), and the known association of JAK inhibitors with cytopenias – the review team recommends including leukopenia and neutropenia as adverse reactions occurring in &lt;1% of subjects in Section 6 of labeling. Since all Grade 3 shifts across all hematologic parameters observed with 52 weeks of continuous treatment were isolated measurements and improved despite continuous treatment, and because of the overall low incidence of the observed cytopenias, baseline CBC and hematologic monitoring is not recommended.
L1395: ## Hyperlipidemia
L1396: Warnings regarding the potential for lipid elevations are included in labeling for JAK inhibitors indicated for the treatment of inflammatory conditions, as JAK inhibition can affect lipid metabolism through interference with cytokine signaling pathways involved in cholesterol and triglyceride regulation. During the delgocitinib clinical trials, the Applicant evaluated lipid profiles periodically under nonfasted conditions to monitor for potential changes in lipid parameters that could indicate emerging hyperlipidemia.
L1397: The review team analyzed subject narratives and shift tables for lipid parameters (triglycerides, cholesterol, LDL, HDL) from baseline to worst value for subjects treated for 16 weeks in phase 3 trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 and for subjects treated continuously with delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g for 52 weeks (refer to Section 7.6.1.6 of this review). Of note, the collection of lipid parameters in a nonfasted state limited the interpretability of these findings and precludes meaningful clinical assessment of lipid changes. In that context, analyses of shift tables, hyperlipidemia-related adverse events, and subject narratives revealed no apparent clinically significant change in lipid parameters in either the placebo-controlled phase 3 safety pool or in the 0-to-52-week long-term exposure pool. Given the limitations described above, potential risks related to JAK inhibition will be included in the Warnings and Precautions section of labeling.
L1398: ## 8. Therapeutic Individualization
L1399: ## 8.1. Intrinsic Factors
L1400: ## Organ Impairment
L1401: Studies in subjects with renal or hepatic impairment were not conducted due to low systemic exposures and the lack of a relationship between plasma concentration and adverse events, including AESIs.
L1402: ## Age
L1403: No studies were conducted to establish safety and efficacy in pediatric patients. Based on the clinical trials, no overall differences in safety or effectiveness were observed between subjects 65 years of age and older and younger adult subjects.
L1404: ## Race
L1405: No studies were conducted to determine therapeutic individualization based on race.
L1406: ## Gender
L1407: No studies were conducted to determine therapeutic individualization based on gender.
L1408: ## Genetics
L1409: The proposed product is intended to be indicated in general population. No genetic factors were considered in the clinical development.
L1410: ## 8.2. Extrinsic Factors
L1411: ## Drug-Drug Interactions
L1412: In vitro drug-drug interaction (DDI) data demonstrated no potential for cytochrome P-450 (CYP)- or transporter-mediated DDIs and therefore, no clinical DDI studies were conducted.
L1413: ## Food
L1414: The drug product is intended to be administered via topical route; therefore, the issue of a food-drug interaction is not relevant.
L1415: ## Alcohol and Smoking
L1416: The drug product is intended to be administered via topical route; therefore, the issue of any alcohol and smoking are not relevant.
L1417: ## 8.3. Plans for Pediatric Drug Development
L1418: To support the evaluation of delgocitinib cream in the pediatric population ages 12 years and older with CHE, the Applicant conducted the following juvenile toxicity studies:
L1419: - Preliminary one-month dermal dose toxicity study in juvenile minipigs (Study 77052).
- Ten-week oral dose toxicity study in juvenile rats with a 12-week recovery period (Study SB95TY).
- Pre- and postnatal developmental toxicity study in rats (Study R-1208).
- Pharmacokinetics in juvenile rats (Study B160168) including metabolite profiling of biological samples (Study JE052PK043).
- Milk excretion in rats (Study B150159).
- Tissue concentrations in pregnant rats (Study B150158).
L1420: In addition, the development program included two juvenile toxicity studies, mass balance in juvenile rats, feto-placental transfer, milk excretion, and a pre- and postnatal developmental toxicity study. No unique or new toxicities were identified in juvenile animals, and the NOAELs in juvenile rats and minipigs were similar to NOAELs obtained in adult animals. Refer to Section 13.1.4.4 Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology.
L1421: The product triggers the Pediatric Research Equity Act as a new active ingredient. The Applicant initiated development of their product in the pediatric population ages 12 to 17 years by conducting a non-IND trial outside of the United States. However, the Applicant experienced challenges in the recruitment of adolescents and requested a deferral of pediatric assessments. Therefore, with this initial NDA submission, the Applicant is seeking approval of delgocitinib cream for the topical treatment of moderate to severe CHE in adults because adult studies are completed and ready for approval. The requested waiver and deferral in the current pediatric plan are the same as the agreed initial pediatric study plan.
L1422: The agreed initial pediatric study plan (dated July 22, 2024) provided for a partial waiver of required pediatric assessments in the pediatric population &lt;12 years of age. Since the number of patients with CHE in this age group is small, studies are impossible or highly impracticable to conduct (Section 505B(a)(4)(B)(i) of the Act). In accordance with 505(a)(d)(A)(ii) of the Pediatric Research Equity Act, the Applicant requested a deferral of the submission of the results of assessments from ongoing Trial 1426 for the treatment of CHE in adolescents aged 12 to 17 years “to avoid delaying the initial NDA submission for the CHE indication in adults.” The Applicant intends to submit the data from Trial 1426 as an efficacy supplement.
L1423: ## 8.4. Pregnancy, Lactation, and Females/Males of Reproductive Potential
L1424: ## 8.4.1. Animal Data
L1425: The following nonclinical information was used in support of product labeling (see Section 13.1.4 for detailed information).
L1426: ## NDA 219155
## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1427: Fertility and early embryonic development (FEED) studies were conducted in male and female rats. In the male FEED study, delgocitinib (0, 3, 10, or 30 mg/kg) was administered daily by oral gavage to male rats for 2 weeks before mating, throughout the mating period, and up to the day before necropsy. In the female FEED study, delgocitinib (0, 3, 10, 30, and 100 mg/kg) was administered daily by oral gavage to female rats for 14 days prior to mating and throughout the mating period to gestation day (GD) 7.
L1428: Embryo-fetal development studies were conducted in rats and rabbits. Delgocitinib (0, 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg) was administered orally to pregnant female rats from GD 7 to 17. On GD 20, all the animals were euthanized for cesarean section and the effects on the embryos/fetuses were examined. Delgocitinib (0, 1, 3, and 10 mg/kg) was administered orally to pregnant female rabbits from GD 6 to 18. On GD 29, all animals were euthanized for cesarean section and the effects on the embryos/fetuses were examined.
L1429: A pre- and postnatal development study was conducted in rats. Delgocitinib (0, 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg) was administered orally to pregnant female rats once daily from GD 7 to lactation day 20, which was the period from implantation to the day before weaning (total maximum dosing period: 37 days).
L1430: Juvenile animal studies were conducted in rats and minipigs. Delgocitinib (0, 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg) was administered orally once daily to juvenile rats (Day 21 of age) for 10 weeks. In a preliminary study in minipigs, delgocitinib was administered by a single intravenous injection (0.1 mg/kg), a single dermal application of JTE-052 ointment (3%), and 28 days of repeated dermal application of JTE-052 ointment (5%) (400 mg/kg ointment/day) (12 and 20 mg/kg/day of JTE-052 for JTE-052 ointment 3% and 5%, respectively) to juvenile minipigs (30 days old).
L1431: A lactation study was conducted to examine the milk secretion of radioactivity in lactating rats. A single dose of ^{14}\text{C}-delgocitinib was administered orally at 1 mg/kg to lactating rats on day 12 postpartum under nonfasting conditions. Radioactivity was excreted in the milk and the area under the concentration-time curve (\text{AUC}_{0-\text{inf}}) of radioactivity in the milk was 2.59 times the value in the plasma. The radioactivity concentrations in the milk decreased over time in a similar manner to those in the plasma.
L1432: The results of these studies are shown in Section 7.1 including NOAELs, systemic exposure, multiples of exposures, and major findings (see Sections 13.1.3 and 13.1.4 for detailed information).
L1433: ## 9. Product Quality
L1434: ## Approval
L1435: The drug substance, delgocitinib, is a white to almost white, nonhygroscopic powder, freely soluble in aqueous solution at pH 3, sparingly soluble at pH 5, and slightly soluble in aqueous solution at pH 7.4. Delgocitinib bulk drug substance is packaged in [REDACTED] with a retest period of [REDACTED] months.
L1436: The Applicant evaluated multiple dosage forms, formulations, and concentrations of delgocitinib during product development. A cream formulation of the proposed product was evaluated in a phase 1 clinical trial, an ointment formulation was evaluated in a phase 2a trial, and a cream formulation was evaluated in phase 2b and 3 trials. Different strengths of delgocitinib cream
L1437: ## NDA 219155
## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1438: were tested in the phase 2b trial, i.e., vehicle, 1 mg/g, 3 mg/g, 8 mg/g and 20 mg/g. For the phase 3 trials, 20 mg/g was the selected strength, which is also the commercial drug product composition. During the course of the phase 2b trial, the final optimization of the formulation was performed and the amount of the [REDACTED] (b) (4) was reduced from [REDACTED] (b) (4) mg/g to [REDACTED] (b) (4) mg/g in phase 3.

L1441: ANZUPGO® (delgocitinib) cream, 2% is packaged with three configurations: 15 g, 30 g and 60 g, in laminated aluminum tubes. The proposed expiry period for the drug product is 36 months when stored at 20°C to 25°C (68°F to 77°F); excursions permitted from 15°C to 30°C (59°F to 86°F) (see United States Pharmacopeia Controlled Room Temperature). Do not freeze.

L1458: The FDA did not hold an Advisory Committee meeting, because the review team identified no unexpected or significant safety/efficacy issue or controversial/challenging issue that would benefit from discussion with external experts. L1459: ## III. Additional Analyses and Information
L1460: ## 12. Summary of Regulatory History
L1461: Delgocitinib, a topical therapeutic agent, was initially synthesized and formulated as an ointment by Japan Tobacco. The compound received regulatory approval in Japan on January 27, 2020, under the proprietary name CORECTIM, for the topical management of atopic dermatitis in both adult and pediatric populations (\ge 6 months of age). In November 2014, Japan Tobacco and LEO Pharma established a licensing agreement, conferring upon LEO Pharma the global rights (excluding Japan) to investigate, develop, and commercialize delgocitinib for topical application in various dermatological conditions.
L1462: LEO Pharma then demonstrated clinical proof of concept for CHE treatment utilizing the ointment formulation in a phase 2a clinical trial. LEO Pharma also developed a cream formulation to help with patient compliance and to enhance the pharmacokinetic profile. This formulation was initially evaluated in a phase 2b trial and subsequently evaluated in multiple clinical trials for CHE, including a phase 1 pharmacokinetic trial and comprehensive phase 3 development program. It should be noted that delgocitinib is also referred to as JTE-052 and LEO 124249 in various scientific and regulatory documentation.
L1463: The U.S. FDA granted fast track designation for the investigation of delgocitinib cream in the treatment of moderate to severe CHE on June 12, 2020, following a fast-track request submission on May 5, 2020. The Applicant originally requested fast track designation on February 17, 2020, but was denied due to the development plan being inadequate. In addition, the Applicant submitted a breakthrough designation request on August 26, 2020, but was denied due to lack of preliminary clinical evidence showing that the drug may demonstrate substantial improvement over existing therapies on one or more clinically significant endpoints.
L1464: Key regulatory interactions with the FDA included:
L1465: - Pre-IND meeting (July 2017; Meeting Minutes dated August 2, 2017)
- IND 135351 submission dated May 25, 2018, and Study May Proceed letter issued on November 2, 2018
- End-of-Phase 2 meetings (December 2019 and September 2020)December 2019 teleconference (Preliminary Comments dated December 2, 2019) related to nonclinical and CMC issues
- September 2020 teleconference (Meeting Minutes dated October 23, 2020)
- December 2020 informal teleconference to discuss phase 3 protocol inclusion of “run-in” period (Memo to File dated January 14, 2021)
- Pre-NDA meeting (September 2023; Meeting Minutes dated October 27, 2023)
L1466: During these meetings, the FDA provided recommendations to the Applicant about various aspects of the clinical development program. At the September 2020 End-of-Phase 2 meeting,
L1467: FDA provided feedback for phase 3 trials DELTA 1 and DELTA 2 and long-term extension trial DELTA 3. The Applicant conducted their phase 3 trials as non-IND trials outside the United States. At the Pre-NDA meeting, the FDA and Applicant agreed on the content and format of the submission of a comprehensive data package to support the proposed NDA.
L1468: The NDA for delgocitinib cream cross-references two INDs: IND 135351 for the cream formulation (submitted May 25, 2018) and IND 128008 for the ointment formulation (submitted October 19, 2015). The cream formulation has been the primary focus of the clinical development program for CHE. The Applicant requested priority review designation with this NDA; however, that request was denied. The Agency denied the priority review request because the Applicant did not provide clinical evidence indicating that the drug demonstrates substantial improvement over existing therapies on one or more clinically significant efficacy or safety endpoints. Therefore, this NDA review followed a standard review clock.
L1469: The Applicant proposed proprietary name ANZUPGO, on July 23, 2024, and on September 23, 2024, this was determined to be conditionally acceptable.
L1470: ## 13. Pharmacology Toxicology
L1471: ## 13.1. Summary Review of Studies Submitted With the Investigational New Drug Application
L1472: The nonclinical studies conducted to support delgocitinib were originally submitted to and reviewed under IND 135351. All pertinent studies were also submitted to the present NDA. Delgocitinib was coded as JTE-052 or JTP-105319 in nonclinical study reports.
L1473: ## 13.1.1. Pharmacology (Primary and Secondary)
L1474: Delgocitinib is a JAK inhibitor that targets the activity of all four members of the JAK family of enzymes, consisting of JAK1, JAK2, JAK3, and TYK2. The established pharmacologic class for delgocitinib is JAK inhibitor. Delgocitinib exhibits inhibitory activity against human JAK1, JAK2, JAK3, and TYK2, thereby inhibiting signal transduction in many inflammatory processes. Delgocitinib inhibits the phosphorylation of STATs stimulated by cytokines IL-2, IL-6, GM-CSF, and IL-23 and inhibits IL-13 and TNF-\alpha production. Delgocitinib inhibits human T cell and B cell proliferation. Tabulated summaries of the in vitro and in vivo pharmacology studies in animals conducted in support of delgocitinib are listed below.
L1475: **Table 83. Summary of In Vitro and In Vivo Pharmacology Studies**
L1476: Study/Study Number	Major Findings

Inhibitory Effect on JAK/10B052EN01	JTE-052 inhibited human recombinant JAK1, JAK2, JAK3 and TYK2 in a concentration-related manner, with IC50 values of 0.00280, 0.00264, 0.0125 and 0.0578\muM/L, respectively.
Inhibitory Effects on Cytokine Signaling/10B052SG01, 11B052SG01	In human PBMCs, JTE-052 inhibited the phosphorylation of STATs stimulated by cytokines (IL-2, IL-6, GM-CSF and IL-

23) in a concentration-related manner. The IC50 values of JTE-052 on IL-2, IL-6, GM-CSF, and IL-23 were 0.0396, 0.0325, 0.304, and 0.0843µM/L, respectively.
In human PBMCs, JTE-052 inhibited the phosphorylation of STAT1 induced by IFN-α in a concentration-related manner. The IC50 value was 0.0181µM/L.

Inhibitory Effect on T Cell Activation and Proliferation Induced by IL-2/10B052HT01, 11B052MT01, 11B052RT01	
JTE-052 inhibited human T cell proliferation in a concentration-related manner, with an IC50 value of 0.0089µM/L.

JTE-052 inhibited rat T cell proliferation in a concentration-related manner with an IC50 value of 0.0153µM/L.

Inhibitory Effect on Mast Cell Activation Induced by IL-4/11B052HH01	
JTE-052 inhibited IL-13 production by human cord blood mast cells in a concentration-related manner, with an IC50 value of 135 nmol/L, demonstrating that JTE-052 inhibits the activation of human mast cells stimulated with IL-4.

Inhibitory Effect on Monocyte Activation Induced by GM-CSF / 11B052HM01	
JTE-052 inhibited TNF-α production from human monocytes stimulated by GM-CSF in a concentration-related manner with an IC50 value of 0.277µM/L.

Effect on The Expression of Skin Barrier Function Related Molecules/11BJAKVT01-02, 11B052HK01	
JTE-052 increased the mRNA levels of filaggrin, loricrin, involucrin, and B-defensin 3 in IL-4/IL-13-stimulated human epidermal keratinocytes in a concentration-related manner. JTE-052 also increased these mRNA levels in unstimulated human epidermal keratinocytes in a concentration-related manner.
JTE-052 suppressed the decrease in the mRNA levels of filaggrin and loricrin in human epidermal keratinocytes stimulated with IL-4 and IL-13 in a concentration-related manner.

In Vivo Pharmacodynamics	
Effect on Dermal Inflammation And Antibody Production/11B052MH01	
JTE-052 inhibited skin inflammation (based on ear thickness) at doses of 3 mg/kg and above in DNFB-induced dermatitis in mice. JTE-052 inhibited the production of total IgE and anti-DNP IgE in this model. JTE-052 decreased the increase in ear tissue cytokine levels at 3 mg/kg and above.

Effect on Dermal Inflammation/13B052RH01	
JTE-052 ointments were considered to inhibit DNCB-induced skin inflammation at 0.3% and above in rats. JTE-052 ointments significantly inhibited ear swelling at concentrations of 0.3% and above and decreased the incidence or severity of histopathological changes in a concentration-related manner.
L1478: Study/Study Number	Major Findings

Mice were treated with IL-31 by single injection into the right cheek. JTE-052 was applied on the right cheek 15 minutes before IL-31 injection. JTE-052 decreased scratching in a concentration dependent manner at concentrations of 0.3% and above.

L1483: ## 13.1.2. Safety Pharmacology
L1484: Delgocitinib:
L1485: - Had no effect on cardiovascular, respiratory, or neurological parameters at clinically relevant doses in rats and dogs.
- Had no inhibitory effect on hERG current at clinically relevant concentrations.
- Had an inhibitory effect on gastrointestinal transport (↓27%) in rats at an oral dose of 30 mg/kg.
L1486: **Table 85. Summary of Safety Pharmacology Studies**
L1487: Study/Study Number	Major Findings
CNS General Condition and Behavior/P100984	JTE-052 was administered orally to rats and effects on the central and the peripheral nervous systems were examined through observation of general condition and behavior. No CNS abnormalities or changes were observed 2-8 hours or 24 hours after administration at any dose.
hERG Current/P100986	JTE-052 did not affect the hERG current at concentrations up to 3×10-6 mol/L.
Blood Pressure, Heart Rate, and ECG/P100985	JTE-052 was administered orally to beagle dogs and effects on cardiovascular parameters (blood pressure, heart rate, and ECG) were investigated. JTE-052 had no effect on blood pressure or heart rate up to 1 mg/kg or on ECG up to 3 mg/kg.
Blood Pressure and Heart Rate/11B052P05	JTE-052 was administered orally to rats and effects on blood pressure and heart rate were examined. JTE-052 at 30 mg/kg had a decreasing effect on blood pressure and an increasing effect on
L1488: Study/Study Number	Major Findings
heart rate in rats. 3 and 10 mg/kg produced no effects attributable to the test article on blood pressure or heart rate.
Vascular Smooth Muscle Contraction/11B052SP01	The effects of JTE-052 on the vascular smooth muscle in rat was assessed using ring preparations of the thoracic aorta. JTE-052 had a direct, reversible relaxant effect on vascular smooth muscle in rat. The IC50 of JTE-052 was 3.6×10-6 mol/L for phenylephrine-induced contraction and 3.5×10-6 mol/L for KCl-induced contraction.
Cardiac Function/11B052SP04	The effects of JTE-052 on cardiac function were evaluated using isolated rat hearts. JTE-052 increased coronary flow at a concentration of 30µM/L, but had no clear effect on cardiac contractility at concentrations up to 30µM/L.
Respiratory Rate, and Blood Gas/P100985	JTE-052 was administered orally to beagle dogs and effects on respiratory parameters (respiratory rate and blood gas) were investigated. JTE-052 had no effect on the respiratory system up to 3 mg/kg.
Gastrointestinal Transport of Charcoal/11B052SP02	The effect of JTE-052 on gastrointestinal transport was investigated in rats. Oral administration of 30 mg/kg JTE-052 had an inhibitory effect on gastrointestinal transport (↓27%) in rats via the gastrointestinal system.
Urine Volume and Urinary Electrolyte Excretion/11B052SP03	The effects of JTE-052 on urine volume and urinary electrolyte (Na+, K+ and Cl-) excretion was investigated in saline-loaded rats. Oral administration of 30 mg/kg JTE-052 had an increasing effect on the urinary potassium excretion via the renal/urinary system.

Plasma Protein Binding	
In Vitro Protein Binding of 14C-JTE-052/B101262	
In vitro protein binding of 14C-JTE-052 was determined in rat, dog, and human plasma. The degrees of plasma protein binding of 14C-JTE-052 in the concentration range of 30 to 100,000 ng/mL were low and comparable: 23.5% to 27.1% in rats, 17.7% to 22.2% in dogs, and 21.8% to 29.1% in humans.

In Vitro Plasma Protein Binding of 14C-JTE-052 in Mice, Rabbits, and Minipigs/B150157	
In vitro protein binding of 14C-JTE-052 was determined in mouse, rabbit, and minipig plasma. The degrees of plasma protein binding of 14C-JTE-052 in the concentration range of 30 to 100,000 ng/mL were low and comparable: 28.4% to 29.4% in mice, 26.1% to 27.5% in rabbits, and 20.7% to 23.0% in minipigs.

Pregnant or Nursing Animal PK	
Feto-Placental Transfer of 14C-JTE-052 in Rats/B150158	
14C-JTE-052 (1 mg/kg) was administered orally to pregnant rats on day 18 of pregnancy. Radioactivity after oral administration of 14C-JTE-052 was transferred into the fetus; however, the radioactivity was not readily distributed to the fetus body due to the placental barrier.
At 0.5-hour postdose, the highest radioactivity concentration in maternal tissue was detected in the small intestine at a level 28.43-fold higher than that in the maternal plasma. Radioactivity concentrations were noted in the kidney, stomach, liver, and adrenal, which were 9.60-, 9.14-, 5.45-, and 3.90-fold higher than that in the maternal plasma, respectively. The highest radioactivity

At 8-hour postdose, the highest radioactivity concentration in maternal tissue was detected in the cecum at a level 220.05-fold higher than that in the maternal plasma. Radioactivity concentrations were noted in the large intestine, small intestine, liver, kidney, adrenal, artery, and pancreas, which were 148.67-, 16.36-, 6.83-, 6.03-, 4.47-, 3.64-, and 3.39-fold higher than that in the maternal plasma, respectively. In fetal tissues, the highest radioactivity concentration was detected in the kidney, followed by the gastrointestinal tract and heart, which were 1.18-, 1.07-, and 1.01-fold that in the maternal plasma.

Milk Secretion of 14C-JTE-052 in Rats/B150159	
14C-JTE-052 (1 mg/kg) was administered orally to lactating rats on day 12 postpartum.
The Cmax in milk was 368.6 ng eq./mL at 0.5-hour postdose (tmax), and the radioactivity concentrations in milk decreased with the t1/2 of 6.3 hr. The AUCinf was 1443 ng eq·hr/mL.
The ratios of milk to plasma (M/P ratios) calculated from the radioactivity concentrations were 1.68, 2.63, 2.73, 3.53, 6.80, and 4.50 at 0.5, 2, 4, 8, 24, and 48-hour postdose, respectively.

Metabolism	
Identification and Characterization of the Metabolites of JTE-052/JE052PK013	
JTE-052 was oxidized at the side chain of the azetidine ring to form M1; JTE-052 was oxidized at the pyrrolo-pyrimidine ring to form M2 or M3; JTE-052 was oxidized at the spiro ring to form M4; and JTE-052 was conjugated to a glucuronide (M5).

Metabolite Profiling of JTE-052 in Human Plasma After Multiple Oral Dose of JTE-052 (Phase 1 Clinical Study of JTE-052 –Single	
The proposed metabolic pathway is shown below.
L1496: Study/Study Number	Major Findings
and Multiple Dose Study–
(NBX1-1))/JE052PK032	
Figure 13. Postulated Metabolic Pathways of Delgocitinib
Source: Pharmacokinetics Written Summary, Study JTE052-AD-016 Study Report
Abbreviation: JTE-052, delgocitinib
The figure illustrates the postulated metabolic pathways of Delgocitinib (JTE-052). The starting compound, JTE-052, undergoes metabolic transformations to form metabolites M1, M2, M3, M4, and M5.
M1 is formed by hydroxylation of the cyclopropyl ring.
M2 is formed by oxidation of the cyclopropyl ring.
M3 is formed by oxidation of the cyclopropyl ring.
M4 is formed by oxidation of the cyclopropyl ring, resulting in a hydroxyl group (O\cdot2H).
M5 is formed by oxidation of the cyclopropyl ring, resulting in a carboxylic acid group (CO\cdotH) and a glucuronide conjugate (GA).

Identification of Human CYP Isoforms Involved in the Metabolism of ^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052/B101265	
^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052 was metabolized by CYP2C19, 2D6, and 3A4. M1, M2 and M3 were formed by CYP3A4, and M3 was formed by CYP2C19 and 2D6. The metabolites generated by CYP3A4 were the same as those formed in the human liver microsomes. No metabolite was detected after incubation with CYP1A2, 2A6, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2E1, or 3A5.
The metabolism of ^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052 and the formation of M1, M2, and M3 in human liver microsomes were completely inhibited by 1\mu\text{M/L} ketoconazole. Sulfaphenazole, (S)-(+)-N-3-benzylnirvanol, and quinidine also inhibited the metabolism of ^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052; however, the inhibition was weak and not concentration-dependent. These results suggest that CYP3A4 plays a major role in the oxidative metabolism of JTE-052.

In Vitro Metabolism Study of ^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052 in Human Skin Microsomes and Recombinant Human CYP Enzymes/B130479	
^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052 was not metabolized by human skin microsomes in the presence or absence of NADPH. ^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052 was metabolized to M3 by CYP1A1. The metabolic degradation rate of ^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052 and the formation rate of M3 during 1 hour incubation was 30.9pM/min/nmol CYP. ^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052 was not metabolized by CYP1B1. These results suggest that CYP1A1 is involved in the oxidative metabolism of JTE-052.

Excretion	
Pharmacokinetics of ^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052 Ointment in Rats After Single Dermal Application/B130379	
^{14}\text{C}-JTE-052 Ointment was applied dermally at 3% (2.4 mg/body).
At 168-hour postdose, 0.82% and 0.44% of the applied radioactivity were excreted into urine and feces, respectively.
Radioactivity in the ointment removed from the application site at 24-hour postdose accounted for 96.84% of the applied radioactivity.

L1500: ## 13.1.4. Toxicology
L1501: ## 13.1.4.1. General Toxicology
L1502: Pivotal 9-month repeat dose toxicity studies were conducted in beagle dogs administered oral delgocitinib (0.1, 0.6, 3.0 mg/kg/day) and in minipigs administered topical delgocitinib (1, 3, 5%). The findings from these pivotal studies are summarized below.
L1503: ## Study Title/Number
L1504: A 9-Month Dermal Toxicity Study of JTE-052 Ointments in Minipigs with a 1-Month Recovery Period / 77057
L1505: Key study findings:
L1506: - The NOAEL was 5% JTE-052 ointment (20 mg/kg/day as a dose level of JTE-052).
- No treatment-related changes were observed in clinical signs, body weights, food consumption, hematology including bone marrow examinations, clinical chemistry, urinalysis, ECG, or ophthalmoscopy during the dosing and recovery periods.
- Skin reactions consisted of very slight to well defined erythema with only a few observations of moderate to severe erythema.
- Plasma concentrations of JTE-052 increased dose-dependently at up to the highest dose level. There were no marked sex differences in the plasma JTE-052 concentrations.

L1513: ## NDA 219155
## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1514: Parameters	Major Findings
Clinical chemistry	No treatment-related changes were observed in clinical chemistry.
Urinalysis	No treatment-related changes were observed in urinalysis.
Gross pathology	Red discoloration of the skin was observed in two animals in Group 4 (5%). Nodules up to 3 mm in diameter were described in two animals in Group 3 (3%) and in one animal from Group 4. Red foci up to 4 mm in diameter were reported in two animals in Group 2 (1%), in seven animals in Group 3 and in six animals in Group 4. In Group 3 and 4, there was an increased incidence and/or severity of epidermal hyperplasia. Infiltration in the epidermis/dermis/subcutaneous tissue of mixed inflammatory cells was observed in several animals in Group 3 and 4 and were considered treatment-related.
Organ weights	Increased weight of the pituitary was observed in Group 4 in animals of both sexes in both absolute values (34%) and as percentage of body weight (24%).
Histopathology
Adequate Battery:
Yes	No treatment-related histopathology findings.
Other Evaluations	Skin reactions consisted of very slight to well defined erythema with only a few observations of moderate to severe erythema. The incidence of erythema was lower in the treated groups (Groups 2, 3 and 4) when compared to the placebo control group (Group 1) in both sexes, indicating a treatment-related effect.

L1525: ## NDA 219155
## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1526: **Table 90. Observations and Results, Study JTE052-TX-033**
L1527: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	Skin lesions during the course of the treatment period at 3.0 mg/kg/day necessitated the early sacrifice of three animals in Week 21.
Clinical signs	Severe skin lesions were observed in HD dogs causing the HD level to be lowered to 1.5 mg/kg/day during Week 24. The cause of the lesions was determined to be Demodex canis (mites). HD dogs were treated with Advocate anti-demodex treatment; however, skin condition of the surviving dogs continued to deteriorate. Dosing was stopped in Week 25 and the 13-week recovery period began at this point for this dose group.
Body weights	No treatment-related changes were observed in body weights.
Ophthalmoscopy	No treatment-related ophthalmic findings.
Electrocardiogram	The electrophysiology of the heart was not affected by treatment.
Hematology	
Reduced lymphocyte counts were observed in LD (18-26%), MD (22-34%), and HD (21-33%) males and HD (13-31%) females starting at Week 13.
Decreased eosinophil counts were observed in weeks 13 (male: -60%; female: -63%), 22 (male: -71%; female: -85%) and 25 (male: -54%; female: -45%) in HD animals and in Week 39 in MD males (-39%).
Increased monocyte counts were observed in HD males (50-108%).
Hematocrit was decreased in Weeks 13 (male: -13%; female: -8%), 22 (male: -6%) and 25 (male -12%; female: -12%) in HD animals.
Hemoglobin concentration was decreased in Weeks 13 (male: -16%; female: -8%), 22 (male: -8%) and 25 (male: -14%; female: -12%) in HD animals.
Erythrocyte count was decreased in Weeks 13 (male: -14%; female: -10%), 22 (male: -5%) and 25 (male: -9%; female: -10%) in HD animals.
An increase in platelet counts was observed in Weeks 13 (male: 26%; female: 28%), 22 (male: 29%; female: 33%) and 25 (male: 43%; female: 40%) in HD animals and in Weeks 13 (16%), 22 (14%), 30 (15%) and 39 (13%) in MD females.

Clinical chemistry	Increased C-reactive protein concentrations were observed in four female HD animals (mean: 197%). Decreased total cholesterol concentrations were observed in Weeks 13 (male: -22%; female: -38%), 22 (male: -29%; female: -26%) and 25 (male: -30%; female: -27%) in HD animals.
Urinalysis	No treatment-related urinalysis findings.
Gross pathology	Hair loss was observed in all HD animals.
Organ weights	No treatment-related organ weight findings
Histopathology	Minimal to slight, multifocal demodicosis was observed in the haired skin (including extremities and/or head) of three recovery HD males and one recovery HD female. Multifocal pyogranulomas/granulomas were associated with mite fragments. Demodex mites were not present within the hair follicles or adnexal glands.
Adequate Battery:
Yes
L1528: Source: JTE052-TX-033 study report
L1529: Abbreviations: HD, high dose; MD, mid-dose
L1530: ## 13.1.4.2. Genetic Toxicology
L1531: **Table 91. Genetic Toxicology Studies**
L1532: Study Title/Number	Major Findings
Bacterial Reverse Mutation Test of JTE-052/11905
GLP compliant	
Tester strains: Salmonella typhimurium (TA98, TA1537, TA100 and TA1535) and Escherichia coli (WP2uvrA)
Highest dose: 5000 µg/plate
Treatment duration: 2 days ± S9 metabolic activation
Delgocitinib was negative for any potential to induce reverse mutations in bacterial tester strains regardless of the presence or absence of S9 mix.

Chromosomal Aberration Test of JTE-052 in Human Peripheral Blood Lymphocytes/TN10466
GLP Compliant	
Test system: Human peripheral blood lymphocytes (2 donors)
Highest dose and treatment duration:
2000 µg/mL, 3-hour-treatment with S9 mix
2000 µg/mL, 3-hour-treatment without S9 mix
200 µg/mL, 24-hour-treatment without S9 mix
Chromosomal numerical aberrations (polyploidy) were significantly increased at concentrations of 125 µg/mL and above after a 3-hour treatment without S9 mix and at concentrations of 50 µg/mL and above after a 24-hour treatment without S9 mix. No increases in the incidence of structural aberrations at any concentrations under either treatment condition were observed.

In Vivo Chromosomal Aberration Test of JTE-052 in Rat Bone Marrow Cells/TN10467
GLP compliant	
Test system: Bone marrow cells from delgocitinib treated rats
Doses and treatment duration: Oral administration to rats at dose levels of 37.5, 75, 150 and 300 mg/kg. Bone marrow chromosomal preparations were made at approximately 24 or 48 hours after dosing.
At 300 mg/kg, 6/12 animals died up to 48 hours after dosing and this dose level was not evaluated for chromosomal aberrations. There were no statistically significant increases in the incidences of chromosomal aberrations (structural aberrations or polyploidy) at any other dose level at 24 or 48 hours posttreatment when compared with those in the negative control groups. JTE-052 was considered not to have any potential to induce chromosomal aberrations (structural aberrations or polyploidy).
The Cmax were 5.237, 8.536 and 13.725 µg/mL at 37.5, 75 and 150 mg/kg, respectively. The AUC0-24hr were 20.659, 52.647 and 123.529 µg·hr/mL at 37.5, 75 and 150 mg/kg, respectively. At 300 mg/kg, the tmax, Cmax, AUC0-24hr and AUC0-48hr were 4.4 hours, 20.582 µg/mL, 285.794 µg·hr/mL, and 346.535 µg·hr/mL, respectively.

Skin Micronucleus Test of JTE-052 in Hairless Mice/G-15-053
Non-GLP	
Test system: Epidermal cells from the skin of male hairless mice administered delgocitinib ointment or solution topically
Doses and duration of treatment: Delgocitinib ointment 3% or delgocitinib ointment 5% (0.035 mL), or 5% solution (0.2 mL) by dermal application to the dorsal skin once daily for 3 days. Animals in all dosing groups were euthanized at 18 to 24 hours after the last dosing and the dorsal skin from the application site was isolated.
There were no increases in the frequency of micronucleated epidermal cells in JTE-052 ointment 3%, JTE-052 ointment 5% or 5% JTE-052 solution groups when compared with the matched control group.
L1533: Source: Nonclinical overview, Toxicology Written and Tabulated Summaries, Individual Study Reports
Abbreviations: AUC0-24hr, area under the concentration-time curve from 0 to 24 hours; AUC0-48hr, area under the concentration-time curve from 0 to 48 hours; Cmax, maximum plasma concentration; GLP, good laboratory practice; JTE-052, delgocitinib; tmax, time to maximum concentration
L1534: ## 13.1.4.3. Special Toxicity Studies
L1535: In a skin irritation study with delgocitinib ointment in rabbits (delgocitinib ointment placebo and 3%), both delgocitinib ointment, 3% and placebo were found to be mild irritants to rabbit skin. In a primary skin irritation study with delgocitinib ointment in minipigs (delgocitinib ointment placebo, 1, and 3%), none of the delgocitinib ointments or placebo were irritant to the minipig skin. The skin reactions observed in the primary skin irritation study in rabbits were considered attributable to (b) (4), the main component of delgocitinib ointment; delgocitinib ointment was considered unlikely to be irritant to the human skin. Delgocitinib cream (2%) administered by dermal application for 28 consecutive days to minipigs was well tolerated and no test item related skin reactions were observed.
L1536: In a BCOP test, delgocitinib ointment placebo, 1%, and 3% were all classified as nonirritants and considered to have no eye irritation potential.
L1537: In a skin sensitization study with delgocitinib ointment in guinea pigs (delgocitinib ointment placebo, 0.3%, 1%, and 3%), delgocitinib ointment did not show skin reactions at any challenge site and was determined to have no skin sensitization potential.
L1538: In a skin photosensitization study with delgocitinib ointment in guinea pigs (delgocitinib ointment placebo, 0.3%, 1%, and 3%), delgocitinib ointment did not show skin reactions at any challenge site with or without ultraviolet irradiation and was determined to have no skin photosensitization potential.
L1539: In a single oral dose phototoxicity study with delgocitinib in mice (dose levels: 25, 50, and 100 mg/kg), no findings suggestive of phototoxicity were observed.
L1540: In a dermal single dose phototoxicity study with delgocitinib ointment in guinea pigs (dose levels: placebo, 0.3%, 1%, and 3%), neither delgocitinib ointment placebo nor delgocitinib 0.3%, 1%, or 3% showed phototoxicity.
L1541: In a 4-week oral dose immunotoxicity study in rats (dose levels: 3, 10 and 30 mg/kg/day), dose-dependent suppression of T cell-dependent antibody response, decreases in all lymphocyte subsets (mainly cytotoxic T lymphocytes and NK cells) in the peripheral blood, and decreases in immature and mature lymphocyte counts in the thymus were observed from the lowest dose level (3 mg/kg) in both sexes. All the changes were considered attributable to the pharmacological action of delgocitinib.

A decrease in fetal weights was observed at 10 mg/kg and above (Males at 10 mg/kg: -5.5%; Males and Females at 30 mg/kg: -16%), and a tendency toward an increase in postimplantation loss index was observed at 30 mg/kg (1.8 times control). There were no treatment-related effects on the number of corpora lutea, the number of implantations, the number of preimplantation losses, the number of live fetuses, the sex ratio, placental weights, or the external or placental findings.
In the visceral examination, an increase in the incidence of thymic remnants in the neck was observed at 30 mg/kg/day.
Skeletal variations included:
Increased incidence of wavy ribs at 10 mg/kg/day and above (0.71%, 0%, 3.75%, and 7.2% at 0, 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg/day, respectively)
Increased incidence of asymmetry of the sternebrae at 30 mg/kg/day (2.14%, 0.71%, 2.89%, and 11.01% at 0, 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg/day, respectively).
Increased incidence of splitting of the sternebrae at 30 mg/kg/day (0%, 0%, 1.43%, and 3.98% at 0, 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg/day, respectively)
Increased incidence of splitting of the thoracic vertebral body at 30 mg/kg/day (0%, 1.25%, 1.25%, and 4.81% at 0, 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg/day, respectively).
Increase incidence of 7 lumbar vertebrae at 30 mg/kg/day (0%, 0%, 0%, and 3.57% at 0, 3, 10, and 30 mg/kg/day, respectively).
At 30 mg/kg, the number of ossified sternebrae was decreased (-9%). Findings in the visceral and skeletal examinations were considered to be changes related to fetal growth retardation because a decrease in fetal weights was observed at 10 mg/kg and above.

L1600: ## NDA 219155
## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1601: Parameters	Major Findings
Observations at delivery and nursing	At 30 mg/kg, statistically significant differences were noted in the following parameters: prolongation of the gestation period (2.3%), a decrease in the birth index (0.8 times control), and an increase in the number of dead newborns (600%). At 30 mg/kg, the number of live newborns (total, both sexes) was lower than that in the control group (~20%). In one female at 30 mg/kg, the time required for delivery was prolonged (the delivery was initiated on GD 22 and completed on GD 24).
Gross pathology	At 30 mg/kg, small thymus and small spleen were noted in 12 of 20 dams and two of 20 dams, respectively.
F1 Offspring
Observation at birth	There were no treatment-related abnormalities in the external examinations in the F1 newborns at any dose level.
Viability	At 30 mg/kg, a significant decrease in the viability index on PND 4 was noted when compared with the control group (0.86 times control). For the weaning index at PND 21, there were no statistically significant differences when compared with the control group.
In one dam at 30 mg/kg, the viability index on PND 4 was very low and the dam had only one live pup (male) on PND 4. The body weight of the live pup (body weight on PND 21: 14 g) was much lower than that of other pups (mean body weight at 30 mg/kg on PND 21: 43.7 g). This pup was found dead on PND 27.
Body weight	At 30 mg/kg, statistically significant decreases in body weights of both sexes were noted after birth until weaning when compared with the control group. After weaning, statistically significant decreases in body weights of both sexes were noted from PND 28 to PND 70 when compared with the control group. In males at this dose level, a statistically significant decrease in total body weight gain from PND 28 to PND 70 was noted when compared with the control group (~11%).
External differentiation	There were no treatment-related effects on external differentiation (pinna detachment, eruption of the lower incisors, opening of eyelids, vaginal opening, or cleavage of the balanopreputial gland) at any dose level.
Sensory and reflex function	There were no treatment-related effects on the reaction time for righting reflex in either sex at any of the treated groups. All pups at all dose levels showed normal reactions for the pupillary reflex, Preyer's reflex, and pain reflex.
Macroscopic findings	At 30 mg/kg, small spleen was noted in twenty-seven of 32 males and twenty-two of 32 females at weaning.
Open field test	There were no treatment-related effects on the time of latency, distance of ambulation, number of rearing or grooming, or fecal boluses in either sex at any dose level.
Rotarod test	There were no treatment-related effects on the counts of falling in either sex at any dose level.
Water-filled multiple T-Maze test	There were no treatment-related effects on the elapsed time and counts of error in either sex at any dose level.
Macroscopic findings after behavioral test	Large spleen was observed in one F1 female at 3 mg/kg and one F1 female at 30 mg/kg. There were no changes in the shape, texture, and color tone of the spleen in these animals.
Organ weights after behavioral test	There were no treatment-related effects on the weights of the thymus or spleen in either sex at any dose level.
Mating and fertility	There were no treatment-related effects on the elapsed day of conceiving, copulation index or fertility index in either sex at any dose level.

Body weights	There were no treatment-related effects on body weights during the gestation period.
