L1612: **Table 103. Observations and Results, Study JTE052-TX-036**
L1613: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	There were five unexpected deaths during the course of the study; none of these deaths were related to JTE-052 administration.
Clinical signs	The primary signs associated with dose administration comprised reddening of the skin for some or all of the extremities, which was observed transiently after dosing.
Body weights	When compared with controls, males and females administered JTE-052 showed a slight and generally dose-dependent reduction in mean body weight gain from Week 2 of treatment (Day 28 of age); this persisted throughout the dosing period for males but was generally apparent in females during Weeks 2-4 and Week 8 of treatment only. Overall mean body weight gain from Days 21-91 of age was lower than the control in all groups of treated males and females, in a statistically significant and dose-dependent manner (males: 97%, 94% and 87% of the control, respectively and females: 94%, 94% and 90% of the control, respectively).
Food consumption	The mean amount of food consumed by males given 10 or 30 mg/kg was slightly higher than the controls during Days 21-27 of age (Week 1) and was similar to the control in Week 2. From Day 35 of age (start of Week 3) there was a decrease in food consumption for males given 30 mg/kg compared to the controls, which persisted to the end of the treatment period. From Days 80-89 of age, mean food consumption was also low for males given 3 or 10 mg/kg when compared to the controls. Among females, at 30 mg/kg mean food consumption was lower than the controls from Day 38 of age to the end of the treatment period. From Days 70-89 of age, food consumption was low for females given 3 or 10 mg/kg when compared to the controls.
During the recovery period, mean food consumption was lower than the controls during Weeks 1-6 for males and females previously given 30 mg/kg, during Weeks 1-4 for males previously given 10 mg/kg, and during Weeks 1-3 for females in the 3 or 10 mg/kg groups. Thereafter, mean food intake was similar to that of the control animals.
L1614: Parameters	Major Findings
Ulna length	
Overall growth of the ulna during the treatment period (Day 21-91 of age) in all treated animals was reduced compared with the controls, with a clear dose response apparent in males. This reduction in ulna growth in both sexes predominantly occurred during the first 6 weeks of treatment (Days 21-63 of age), with growth being similar to the control during the final 4 weeks of the dosing period.
Following the cessation of treatment, overall mean growth of the ulna during the first 6 weeks of the recovery period (Day 91-133 of age; prior to the mating trial for the Reproductive phase animals) was greater than the controls for all the groups of previously treated males and for females previously given 30 mg/kg, although the ulna length in males at 30 mg/kg at the end of the recovery period was still shorter than that in the control group. Thereafter, from Day 133 of age until termination of the Recovery phase and Immunology phase animals, mean ulna growth was similar to the controls.

Urinalysis	
A non-dose-dependent decrease in urine volume was observed in males given 30 mg/kg. Urinary output of electrolytes (sodium, potassium, and chloride) was decreased in all the groups of treated males and females. The decrease in sodium concentration was seen in males given 30 mg/kg, decreased potassium concentration was seen in males at all dose levels and females at 10 or 30 mg/kg, and low chloride concentration was seen in males given 10 or 30 mg/kg.
The analysis of urinary parameters during Week 12 of the recovery period did not reveal any differences from the controls, indicating full recovery.

Blood chemistry	
Biochemical examination of plasma performed after 10 weeks of treatment showed, when compared with the controls, decreases in mean plasma urea in males and females given 30 mg/kg and creatinine in females given 10 or 30 mg/kg. Cholesterol concentrations were increased in females given 30 mg/kg. Sodium concentrations were high in females in all the treated groups and chloride concentrations were high in females given 10 or 30 mg/kg. A decrease in plasma calcium concentration was seen in females given 10 or 30 mg/kg and phosphorus concentration was high in females given 30 mg/kg. There was no effect on plasma electrolytes in the males.
Further biochemical examination of plasma performed after 12 weeks of the recovery period did not reveal any differences from the controls, indicating full recovery.
L1615: Parameters	Major Findings
Hematology	
Analysis of the erythrocytic parameters after 10 weeks of treatment showed, when compared with the controls, a decrease in hematocrit and hemoglobin concentrations and low erythrocyte counts in females given 30 mg/kg. Red cell distribution width was high in both sexes at all dose levels with the exception of females at 3 mg/kg and a clear dose-relationship apparent in the females. Erythrocyte counts were low in males previously given 10 or 30 mg/kg. The analysis of erythrocytic parameters during Week 8 of the recovery period and at termination of the recovery period did not reveal any differences from the controls, indicating full recovery.
Assessment of leukocytic parameters after 10 weeks of treatment revealed, when compared to the controls, low lymphocyte and basophil counts in all the treated groups of males and females, low large unstained cell counts in males given 10 or 30 mg/kg and in all the groups of treated females, and low eosinophil counts for all the groups of treated males and for females given 30 mg/kg. There was a decrease in neutrophil counts for males given 30 mg/kg. Monocyte counts were low in males and females given 10 or 30 mg/kg. These changes resulted in low total leukocyte counts in both sexes at all dose levels, with the magnitude of the change demonstrating a clear relationship to treatment.
Further investigations performed during Week 4 of the recovery period revealed low lymphocyte and basophil counts in males previously given 3, 10 or 30 mg/kg. Total leukocyte counts were decreased in males at all dose levels. The analysis performed at termination of the recovery phase did not reveal any differences from the control and it is therefore considered that full recovery from the treatment-related changes in leukocytic parameters had occurred.
The examination of platelet counts and clotting times performed after 10 weeks of treatment and after 12 weeks of the recovery period did not reveal any test article-related differences from the control.

Hematology, bone marrow	
The examination of the bone marrow performed after 10 weeks of treatment revealed, when compared with the controls, an increase in total erythroid cell ratio in both sexes at 10 mg/kg and above and in females at 3 mg/kg, and a decrease in the total other cell ratios for males at 10 mg/kg and above and for all the groups of treated females, with a statistical significance in males at 30 mg/kg and in females at all dose levels.
The analysis of the bone marrow at termination of the recovery period did not reveal any differences from the controls, indicating full recovery.

Organ weights	
The analysis of organ weights performed for the Main phase animals killed after 10 weeks of treatment revealed, when compared with the controls, low absolute adrenal gland and spleen weights in males and females at all dose levels. When considered relative to terminal body weight, changes in adrenal gland weights were limited to males at 10 mg/kg and above and in females at 30 mg/kg, but changes in spleen weights were apparent for both sexes in all the treated groups. Low thymus weights were seen in males and females given 10 or 30 mg/kg.

Gross pathology	
No macroscopic findings were seen in any animal at any dose.
L1616: Parameters	Major Findings
Histopathology	
Histopathological changes related to treatment with JTE-052 occurred in the thymus, spleen, bone marrow (sternum and femur including the joint) and adrenal glands.
A decrease in cellular density and size of the cortex of the thymus was observed in animals of both sexes treated with 30 mg/kg and in one male treated with 10 mg/kg. Similar changes were observed in the medulla of males and females at 10 mg/kg and above with a dose-response pattern. The changes of cellular density/size of the cortex and medulla correlated with a reduction in organ weight.
A decreased size of the PALS and marginal zones in the spleen was observed in both sexes given 10 mg/kg and above, with a dose-response pattern, correlating with a reduction of organ weight.
Changes in the bone marrow consistent with increased adipocytes were observed in the sternum and femur in males at all dose levels. In females, similar changes were observed in the sternum at 10 mg/kg and above and in the femur at 30 mg/kg only.
Minimal hypertrophy of the zona glomerulosa was observed in both sexes at 30 mg/kg; affected cells appeared larger with pale/foamy cytoplasm.

Immunotoxicology	
The following changes in the immunophenotyping parameters were observed at the end of the dosing period: decreases in the absolute counts of T, B and NK cells in both sexes at all dose levels, a dose-dependent decrease in CD8+ T cells in both sexes at all dose levels, and dose-dependent decreases in the proportions of NK cells and CD8+ T cells in both sexes at all dose levels. The proportion of CD4+ T cells also showed a concomitant increase; however, as the absolute count of this population did not change, this is expected to be a result of the decrease in the CD8+ T cells. These changes had recovered at Week 4 of the recovery period and no further changes were observed in subsequent recovery period occasions.
In the TDAR assessment at the end of the dosing period, the levels of IgM at Day 84 of age (primary response) and the levels of IgG or IgM at Day 91 of age (secondary response) decreased dose-dependently in both sexes at all dose levels when compared with the control group. This was not seen at the end of the recovery period (Day 175).

Sexual maturation	
The age and body weights of the Reproductive phase animals at attainment of vaginal opening and balanopreputial separation were not affected by treatment with JTE-052.

Estrous cycle, precoital interval, mating performance and fertility	
There was no effect of treatment with JTE-052 upon estrous cycle regularity during Week 6-7 of treatment or during Week 5-6 of the recovery period (prior to pairing for mating). The majority of females in all the groups demonstrated a regular 4- to 5-day cycle.
In order to establish the reproductive capacity of the Reproductive phase males and females which had previously received JTE-052, the animals were paired with naive animals (study males with untreated females, study females with stock males) after at least 6 weeks recovery. All mating pairs showed positive evidence of mating at the first estrus opportunity after pairing, and the conception and fertility indices among males and females previously treated with JTE-052 were 96-100%.
L1617: Parameters	Major Findings
Reproductive assessment	
There was no indication of any effect of previous treatment of males with JTE-052 on their reproductive performance as judged by the litter size and survival of the conceptuses in the litters of their untreated female partners.
There was no effect of previous treatment of females with JTE-052 on their reproductive performance, as supported by the fact that treatment was not associated with any delay in the timing of female sexual maturity or regularity of estrous cycles during treatment, on female reproductive organ weights, or the macroscopic or microscopic appearance of female reproductive organs, and that there was no effect of previous treatment on estrous cycles, mating performance and fertility during recovery.
L1618: Source: Study JTE052-TX-036 Study Report
L1619: Abbreviations: CD, cluster of differentiation; Ig, immunoglobulin; JTE-052, delgocitinib; NK, natural killer; PALS, periarterial lymphoid sheath; TDAR, T-dependent antibody response
L1620: ## Study Title/Number
L1621: ## Preliminary One-Month Dermal Dose Toxicity Study of JTE-052 ointment in Juvenile Minipigs with Pharmacokinetic Analyses/77052
L1622: Juvenile Göttingen minipigs (30 days of age, 3 males) were administered a single intravenous dose of delgocitinib at 0.1 mg/kg. For the general toxicity investigation, a single dose of delgocitinib ointment (3%) at a dose volume of 400 mg/kg (12 mg/kg) was applied dermally to clipped dorsal skin (approximately 10% BSA) of the same animals (32 days of age) on Day 3 and repeated doses of delgocitinib ointment (5%) at a dose volume of 400 mg/kg (20 mg/kg) were then applied dermally to the clipped dorsal skin (approximately 10% BSA) of the same animals (35 days of age) once daily for 28 days starting on Day 6 (on Days 6 to 33).
L1623: - The NOAEL was determined as 5% delgocitinib ointment.
- There were no findings considered related to treatment with JTE-052 in the skin reactions, clinical signs, body weights or clinical pathology throughout the dosing period.
- In the single intravenous injection of JTE-052 at 0.1 mg/kg, the mean AUC0-last was 134.2 ng.hr/mL.
- Exposure after a single dermal application (plasma concentration 24 hours postdosing) appeared to increase proportional to dose level.
- In the single dermal dose of JTE-052 ointment 3%, plasma JTE-052 concentrations were very low (below or close to LLOQ: 0.5 ng/mL) for up to 24 hours after dose.
- After 28 days' repeated dermal dosing of JTE-052 ointment 5%, plasma JTE-052 concentrations were very low (below or close to LLOQ) for all JTE-052 treated animals.
L1624: ## 13.1.5. Excipients/Impurities
L1625: There are no novel excipients in the clinical formulation. All the inactive ingredients are below approved levels for the dermal route of administration listed in the FDA's inactive ingredient database. The Applicant provided an impurity characterization and control strategy, or the impurities have been qualified in studies described below. The potential impurities that are evaluated and controlled include product-related impurities, process-related impurities, (b) (4)
L1626: (b) (4), elemental impurities, and contaminants. The proposed control strategy is considered acceptable. There are no safety concerns for potential impurities contained in this product.
L1627: Sprague Dawley rats were administered delgocitinib + impurities (30 mg/kg/day) by oral gavage for 4 weeks in a study investigating the toxicity of impurities in the delgocitinib drug substance. The impurities included (b) (4) each at a dose of (b) (4) mg/kg/day (b) (4) of each impurity; 27 mg delgocitinib + (b) (4) mg impurities). Delgocitinib + impurities at 27+(b) (4) mg/kg/day to Sprague-Dawley rats administered for 4 weeks resulted in nonadverse changes in the spleen, thymus and bone marrow, associated changes in organ weights, and changes in leukocyte counts and erythrocyte indices. All findings were considered to be related to JAK inhibition, and there were no significant differences in the findings between animals that were treated with delgocitinib alone or with delgocitinib + impurities, which indicates that the impurities administered at dose levels of (b) (4) mg/kg/day (corresponding to approximately (b) (4)% in the formulation) induced no additional effects or systemic toxicity.
L1628: The (b) (4) was shown in PK studies to be present at higher amounts in nonclinical toxicology studies following metabolism of the parent compound than in human studies. This qualifies (b) (4) at the proposed \le (b) (4)% release limit and \le (b) (4)% shelf-life limit.
L1629: The (b) (4) impurity (b) (4) has a proposed acceptable intake (AI) of (b) (4) ng/day. (b) (4) was determined as nonmutagenic in an Ames assay in five strains (TA98, TA100, TA1535, TA1537 and TA102) of *Salmonella typhimurium* at concentrations up to 5000 \mug/plate. The Center for Drug Evaluation and Research/Office of Translational Sciences/Office of Clinical Pharmacology/Division of Applied Regulatory Science Computational Toxicology Consultation Service was consulted to apply the Carcinogenic Potency Categorization Approach to calculate the carcinogenic potency category (PC) and associated AI limit of (b) (4) as well. (b) (4) is a (b) (4) form of the active pharmaceutical ingredient, delgocitinib. The Computational Toxicology Consultation Service concluded that (b) (4) is assigned to PC 5 and has an associated recommended AI limit of (b) (4) ng/day, which agrees with the calculated PC and AI limit.
L1630: ## 13.1.6. Extractables/Leachables
L1631: The container closure system for the drug product, consisting of 15 g, 30 g and 60 g tubes, is a laminate tube with an inner layer of (b) (4) fitted with a (b) (4) flip-top cap.
L1632: An extractables study was completed using the 15 g tube size of the container closure system. The tube, with seal and cap, was filled with 15 mL simulation solvent and stored for 7 months at 40°C. The simulation solvent was chosen to mimic the extractable power of the drug product; hence, a combination of water and organic solvents was used in combination with acid to mimic the drug product. To mimic the acidity of the drug product formulation in the simulation solvent, (b) (4) used for drug product vehicle composition was used. The maximum daily dose (MDD) for the drug product is (b) (4). The mean amount of cream used in the phase 3 clinical study was 1.4 g/day. Twice-daily treatment is not expected to continue on
L1633: ## NDA 219155
## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1634: a long-term basis, but a duration of more than 10 years for the treatment of CHE (as for atopic dermatitis) is considered a conservative worst-case duration.
L1635: Justification of the levels of extractables and leachables found in the study are shown below.
L1636: ## Unidentified Impurities
L1637: Two unidentified compounds were detected in tube samples of the container closure system above the safety control threshold (SCT) of 1.5 \mu\text{g}/\text{day}. The estimated amounts of these unidentified compounds in the tube samples are (b)(4)\mu\text{g}/\text{tube} and (b)(4)\mu\text{g}/\text{tube}, respectively.
L1638: ## Equation 1. Unknown #1 MDE
L1639: (b)(4)\mu\text{g impurity}/\text{tube in } 15 \text{ g tube} = (b)(4)\mu\text{g impurity}/\text{g product} \times 1.4 \text{ g}/\text{day} = (b)(4)\mu\text{g impurity}/\text{day}
L1640: Abbreviation: MDE, maximum daily exposure
L1641: ## Equation 2. Unknown #2 MDE
L1642: (b)(4)\mu\text{g impurity}/\text{tube in } 15 \text{ g tube} = (b)(4)\mu\text{g impurity}/\text{g product} \times 1.4 \text{ g}/\text{day} = (b)(4)\mu\text{g impurity}/\text{day}
L1643: Abbreviation: MDE, maximum daily exposure
L1644: Both impurities are below the 1.5 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} SCT at both the MDD and normal use doses. These impurities do not present a significant toxicological risk at these levels in the final drug product.
L1645: (b)(4) categorized as Cramer Class II in a quantitative structure-activity relationship assessment, indicating a potential for moderate skin irritation and sensitization, was identified as a potential contaminant from packaging materials at a maximum concentration of (b)(4)\mu\text{g}/\text{tube}. Based on the Product Quality Research Institute recommendations for parenteral drug products, irritants and sensitizers have an SCT of 5 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} maximum daily exposure (MDE).
L1646: ## Equation 3. (b)(4) MDE
L1647: (b)(4)\mu\text{g} (b)(4)\text{tube in } 15 \text{ g tube} = (b)(4)\mu\text{g} (b)(4)/\text{g cream} \times 1.4 \text{ g}/\text{day} = (b)(4)\mu\text{g} (b)(4)\text{day}
L1648: Abbreviation: MDE, maximum daily exposure
L1649: The MDE at the mean phase 3 dose is below the Product Quality Research Institute SCT of 5 \mu\text{g}/\text{day}. Based on this, the level of (b)(4) is acceptable.
L1650: (b)(4) is a Class 2 mutagenic impurity formed from (b)(4) present in the excipient (b)(4). The excipient contains (b)(4)\text{ppm} (b)(4)\text{%} of (b)(4) resulting in a concentration of (b)(4)\text{ppm} of (b)(4) in the final formulation. The acceptable limit for (b)(4) in the cream is (b)(4)\text{ppm}. Theoretically, if all (b)(4) were to convert to (b)(4) it would exceed the acceptable limit. The (b)(4)\mu\text{g}/\text{g} limit for (b)(4) is based on the MDD of (b)(4)\text{day} of the drug product.
L1651: ## NDA 219155
## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1652: At the mean clinical use dose of 1.4 g/day of the drug product, the limit at a 1.5 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} acceptable intake would be 1.1 \mu\text{g}/\text{g}.
L1653: ## **Equation 4.** (b) (4)
L1654: (b) (4) \mu\text{g} (b) (4) /g cream = (b) (4) \mu\text{g} (b) (4) /g cream in a worst-case scenario
L1655: (b) (4) \mu\text{g} (b) (4) /g cream \times 1.4 g cream/day = (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{day}
L1656: Abbreviation: MDE, maximum daily exposure
L1657: The development of (b) (4) has been monitored during the first weeks/months after manufacture. The (b) (4) level is below the limit of (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{g} after about 1 month at long-term storage conditions. All stability datapoints, for up to 36 months at long-term and 6 months at accelerated storage conditions, show (b) (4) levels below the (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{g} limit. During the first month after manufacture, varying levels of (b) (4) between (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{g} are observed, all well below the limit of (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{g} applied for a treatment duration of more than 10 years. Based on the available data, an (b) (4) limit of (b) (4) ppm is estimated to generate a peak level of (b) (4) below (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{g}. The concentration of (b) (4) would then rapidly decrease to a level below the ICH M7 (R2) (April 2023) limit of (b) (4) \mu\text{g}/\text{g}. When the content of (b) (4) decreases, (b) (4), a Class 5 impurity, is formed. (b) (4) is controlled according to the specification limit for any unspecified organic impurities. The toxicological assessment of (b) (4) showed no risk associated with the observed level because of rapid degradation of (b) (4) in blood and skin to nonmutagenic metabolites, and it is therefore highly unlikely that (b) (4) will be present in the body to generate any mutagenic or carcinogenic effects. When applied topically, degradation of (b) (4) will take place before it reaches the viable epidermis (with a pH of approximately 7) as the degradation is pH-dependent. Additionally, based on the timeframe for release and distribution of the drug product, it is unlikely that patients would receive the drug product within a month of its manufacture. Based on the factors of degradation of (b) (4) in long-term storage, topical degradation, the low likelihood of total conversion of (b) (4) to (b) (4) and the acceptable ppm and MDE levels compared to limits at the mean clinical dose, the level of (b) (4) is acceptable.
L1658: (b) (4)
L1659: The estimated total (b) (4) content in the drug product is (b) (4) ppm. This level exceeds the calculated limit of (b) (4) ppm based on the acceptable daily intake for Class 3 impurities as per ICH M7(R2) (April 2023).
L1660: ## **Equation 5.** (b) (4) MDE
L1661: (b) (4) \mu\text{g} (b) (4) g cream \times 1.4 g/day cream = (b) (4) \mu\text{g} (b) (4) \text{day}
L1662: Abbreviation: MDE, maximum daily exposure

Lung: White focus was increased in males at 30 mg/kg/day and females at 3 mg/kg/day and above. The majority of the white foci corresponded to alveolar proteinosis histopathologically.
Eye: Opacity was increased in males at 30 mg/kg/day. Opacity corresponded to corneal inflammation histopathologically.

Histopathology	
The only tumor finding with statistically significant findings in both the pairwise comparison and dose relationship trend data was benign thymoma in the thymus of females at the high dose of 30 mg/kg/day.
Following the multiple testing adjustment method, analysis showed statistically significant increasing dose-response relationships across the vehicle control and the treated groups in male rats for the incidence of malignant astrocytoma in the brain (cerebrum) and in female rats for the incidence of the benign thymoma in the thymus (p-value=0.0089 and =0.0006, respectively). The pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increases in the medium dose group, for the incidences of adenoma hepatocellular in the liver and the incidence of the lipoma in the skin/subcutis, in male rats, when compared to the vehicle control group (p-value=0.0078 and =0.0078, respectively). In female rats, the pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increases in the medium dose group for the incidences of the malignant astrocytoma in

the brain (cerebrum), and in the high-dose group for the incidence of benign thymoma in the thymus, when compared to the vehicle control group (p-value=0.0384 and =0.0072, respectively).

Lung: The incidence and severity of alveolar proteinosis were increased in both sexes at 3 mg/kg/day and above.
Eye: The incidence and severity of corneal inflammation were increased in males at 30 mg/kg/day. Moderate and severe inflammation was observed in males at 30 mg/kg/day. In most cases including control animals, the lesion was unilateral, not bilateral.
Skin/subcutis: The incidence of increased dermal adipose tissue in the inguinal region was increased in males at 10 mg/kg/day and above and in females at 3 mg/kg/day and above, although there was no dose response in females. Pyogranulomatous inflammation was observed in both sexes at 30 mg/kg/day.
Liver: The incidence of clear altered cell foci was increased in females at 3 mg/kg/day and above; however, the severity in most of the foci was very slight and there was no apparent dose response.
Seminal vesicle: The incidence of vesicular dilatation was increased in males at 10 mg/kg/day and above.
Kidney: Intraluminal mineralization was observed in males at 10 mg/kg/day and above and females at 3 mg/kg/day and above. This lesion was mainly observed in animals that died or were euthanized in moribund condition.

Mesenteric lymph node: The incidence and severity of lymphoid atrophy were increased in males at 30 mg/kg/day and females at 10 mg/kg/day and above.
Submandibular lymph node: Lymphoid atrophy was increased in females at 30 mg/kg/day.
Thymus: The severity of atrophy was increased in both sexes at 30 mg/kg/day.

L1703: ## 14. Clinical Pharmacology
L1704: ## 14.1. In Vitro Studies
L1705: ## Metabolism
L1706: Limited metabolism of delgocitinib was observed in vitro and in vivo with no major human-specific metabolites. Delgocitinib was the main component in human plasma and in the plasma, urine, bile, and feces of rats and dogs. No metabolites were detected in plasma from juvenile rats. Metabolism occurred by oxidation and through glucuronide conjugation.
L1707: Delgocitinib was mainly metabolized by CYP3A4/5 and with minor contribution from CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP2D6.
L1708: ## In Vitro Drug Interaction Assessments
L1709: Delgocitinib was evaluated for potential drug interactions across series of CYP enzymes and transporters under in vitro conditions. The key findings from the reported in vitro studies are summarized below.
L1710: ## **Delgocitinib as Inhibitor of CYP (Study JE052-PK-021)**
L1711: Delgocitinib was investigated for inhibitory effects on human CYP isoform-specific activities of CYP1A2 (phenacetin), CYP2A6 (coumarin), CYP2B6 (bupropion), CYP2C8 (amodiaquine), CYP2C9 (diclofenac), CYP2C19 (mephenytoin), CYP2D6 (bufuralol), and CYP3A4/5 (testosterone and midazolam) at concentrations of 0, 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, 10, and 30 \muM/L using human liver microsomes. The time-dependent inhibitory effect of delgocitinib was also evaluated after preincubation with microsomes for 30 minutes in the presence of NADPH.
L1712: The results indicated that the IC50 of delgocitinib was &gt;30\muM/L for all the CYP isoforms investigated. After preincubation for 30 minutes, the extent of inhibition was almost the same as that without preincubation for all the CYP isoforms examined, indicating no potential for time-dependent inhibition of the tested CYP isoforms.
L1713: ## **Delgocitinib as Inducer of CYP (Study JTE052-AD-014)**
L1714: The study was designed to evaluate the CYP-inducing potential of delgocitinib using fresh human hepatocytes. The effects of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, or CYP3A4 activity was studied in the fresh preparations of cultured human hepatocytes from three separate livers treated once daily for 3 consecutive days with dimethyl sulfoxide (DMSO, 0.1% v/v, vehicle control), one of five concentrations of delgocitinib (0.3, 1, 3, 10, or 30\muM) or one of three known human CYP
L1715: NDA 219155
L1716: ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1717: inducers, namely, omeprazole (50\mu\text{M}), phenobarbital (750\mu\text{M}), and rifampin (10\mu\text{M}) and with the marker substrates for the analysis of phenacetin O-deethylation (marker for CYP1A2), bupropion hydroxylation (marker for CYP2B6), and testosterone 6\beta-hydroxylation (marker for CYP3A4/5).
L1718: The results showed that delgocitinib, at concentrations up to 30\mu\text{M}, is not an inducer of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, or CYP3A4 activity or mRNA expression. It is noted that concentration-dependent decreases were observed in CYP3A4/5 activity; however, since this drug is applied topically at the target site (skin), the potential effect of enzyme induction on the systemic exposure of delgocitinib is unlikely to cause any effect on efficacy.
L1719: ## Transporter Study (Study JTE052-AD-015)
L1720: The study was designed to investigate the potential of delgocitinib as a substrate or inhibitor of P-glycoprotein (P-gp) and breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP) and substrate/inhibitor of organic cation transporter (OCT)2, OAT1, and OAT3. The inhibitory effect of delgocitinib on uptake of model substrates in OAT1- and OAT3-expressing S2 cells and OCT2-, organic anion transporting polypeptide (OATP)1B1- and OATP1B3-expressing HEK293 cells was also investigated to examine whether delgocitinib was an inhibitor of OAT1, OAT3, OCT2, OATP1B1, or OATP1B3.
L1721: The results showed that delgocitinib is a substrate of P-gp but not a substrate of BCRP and not an inhibitor of P-gp or BCRP, and is a weak substrate of OCT2 and OAT3, but not a substrate of OAT1. In addition, delgocitinib showed inhibition of OAT1 and OAT3 with \text{IC}_{50} values of 1.59\mu\text{M/L} (493 ng/mL) and 6.38\mu\text{M/L} (1980 ng/mL), respectively. Due to the low systemic exposure observed in the maximal use study in subjects with CHE, there is no concern for delgocitinib to inhibit OAT1 and OAT3. Delgocitinib exhibited little to no inhibition of the OATP1B1, OATP1B3 and the OCT2-mediated uptakes, indicating that delgocitinib showed no inhibition of OCT2, OATP1B1, or OATP1B3.
L1722: ## Solute Carrier Transporter Potential in Cells (Study (b)(4) 208059)
L1723: The study was designed to evaluate inhibitory and substrate potential of delgocitinib of the human transporters multidrug and toxin extrusion (MATE)1 and MATE2-K.

L1732: The results showed that delgocitinib is not an inhibitor or substrate of MATE1 or MATE2-K.
L1733: Overall, based on the in vitro DDI studies, no DDI potential of delgocitinib is observed and therefore, the Applicant did not perform any clinical drug interaction studies. The Applicant's justification is that as delgocitinib showed limited metabolism, and low systemic exposure of topically applied delgocitinib, a low potential for any drug interaction is anticipated. The Applicant's justifications are reasonable.
L1734: ## 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L1735: Delgocitinib is a pan-JAK inhibitor and acts on the JAK family of enzymes (JAK1, JAK2, JAK3), and TYK2 in a concentration-dependent manner. JAKs are intracellular enzymes associated with different cytokine receptors (heterodimeric or homodimeric complex) and are essential for cytokine signaling. Activation of JAKs upon cytokine-receptor interaction leads to initiation of phosphorylation and activation of STATs. The activated STATs activate the expression of cytokine-responsive genes to induce specific biological responses in target cells. Delgocitinib, by inhibiting the JAK-STAT pathway, attenuates the signaling of proinflammatory cytokines (IL-2, IL-4, IL-6, IL-13, IL-21, IL-23, GM-CSF), and IFN-\alpha, downregulating the immune and inflammatory responses in cells of relevance to CHE pathology. The proposed indication for delgocitinib cream is for the topical treatment of moderate-to-severe CHE in adults who have had an inadequate response to, or for whom topical corticosteroids are not advisable.
L1736: The Applicant's clinical development program involved:
L1737: - Five phase 1 trials (four studies in healthy volunteers, one in adults, adolescents/children with moderate-to-severe AD (1181) (maximal use trial [MuST], one trial in subjects with moderate-to-severe CHE [MuST], and one trial in adults, adolescents, and children with moderate-to-severe atopic dermatitis).
- Three phase 2 trials (two in subjects with mild-to-severe CHE and one in subjects with AD).
- Three phase 3 trials in subjects with moderate-to-severe CHE (DELTA 3) along with two phase 3 ongoing trials in subjects with moderate-to-severe CHE.
L1738: ## NDA 219155
## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1739: The pharmacokinetics of delgocitinib was assessed in the following trials:
L1740: - Phase 1 trial under maximal use conditions with twice-daily administration of delgocitinib cream to evaluate pharmacokinetics and safety in adult subjects with moderate-to-severe CHE (LP0133-2285).
- Phase 3 clinical trial to assess efficacy and evaluate safety for 16 weeks in adult subjects with moderate-to-severe chronic hand eczema (DELTA 2).
- Phase 1 trial under maximal use conditions in subjects with AD (LP0133-1181) for 8 weeks in adults, adolescents, and children with moderate-to-severe AD.
- Phase 2a trial with twice-daily applications of delgocitinib ointment 30 mg/g in the treatment of chronic hand eczema (LP0133-1180).
- Phase 2b dose-ranging trial to evaluate delgocitinib cream for 16 weeks in adult subjects with chronic hand eczema (LP0133-1273).
- Phase 2b dose-ranging trial for 8 weeks in adult subjects with atopic dermatitis (LP0133-1275).
- Phase 1 trial to evaluate QT interval using Fridericia's correction (QTcF) prolongation and proarrhythmic potential evaluation for delgocitinib following oral administration in healthy subjects (LP0133-1409).
- Phase 1 trial to evaluate oral pharmacokinetics of delgocitinib in healthy subjects as single and multiple doses (NBX1-1).
L1741: ## **Recommendation**
L1742: NDA 219155 is acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective.
L1743: ## **Postmarketing Commitment/PMR**
L1744: None.
L1745: ## **Summary of Clinical Pharmacology**
L1746: The summary of pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, and pharmacokinetic/pharmacodynamic relationships of delgocitinib provided by the Applicant is based on the following clinical pharmacology studies conducted in subjects with CHE and AD:
L1747: - Maximal use trial in adult subjects with moderate-to-severe CHE with final formulation (LP0133-2285).
- Maximal use trial in pediatric and adult subjects with AD (LP0133-1181) with earlier formulation.
- Phase 3 trial in adult subjects with moderate-to-severe CHE (DELTA 2) with the final formulation (subgroup of subjects treated under maximal use conditions).
L1748: NDA 219155
L1749: ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1750: ## Phase 1 Trial in Subjects With Moderate-to-Severe CHE (LP0133-2285)
L1751: ## Trial Design
L1752: This was a phase 1, open-label, single-site trial to evaluate the pharmacokinetics and safety of delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g (2%) in subjects with moderate-to-severe CHE.
L1753: **Figure 14. Design for Phase 1 Pharmacokinetic Trial LP0133-2285**
L1754: Source: Panel 2, Page 16 of 146, clinical study report LP0133-2285
L1755: Abbreviations: IMP, investigational medicinal product; N, number of subjects, h, hours; PK, pharmacokinetic
L1756: ## Trial Population
L1757: The trial was conducted in adult subjects (N=15; age range of 22 to 69 years) with moderate-to-severe CHE (according to IGA-CHE [i.e., an IGA-CHE score of 3 or 4]) who have had an inadequate response to, or for whom topical corticosteroids were medically inadvisable. The median BSA was 1%, range 0.25 to 3%.
L1758: ## Formulation
L1759: The formulation of delgocitinib cream, 20 mg/g (2%), was the same as the final formulation. The product was applied as a thin layer covering the affected areas, twice daily for 1 week (Days 1 to 7 plus one application on Day 8). The average IMP use during the 1-week treatment period was 13.07 g which corresponds to 0.87 g per application (15 applications in total during the trial) (Table 1.4.1: Summary of Exposure, Page No 61 of 146, clinical trial report LP0133-2285).
L1760: ## Pharmacokinetic Assessment
L1761: Pharmacokinetic assessment was performed on Day 1 and Days 8 to 11; Day 1: 0 hour (predose), 1 hour, 2 hours, 4 hours, 8 hours, 12 hours post-morning dose and Days 8 to 11: 0 hour (predose), 1 hour, 2 hours, 4 hours, 8 hours, 12 hours, 24 hours, 36 hours, 48 hours, 72 hours post-Day 8 morning dose.

L1763: The PK results and demographics are shown below.

ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1777: ## Safety Assessments
L1778: Delgocitinib was well-tolerated and there were no AEs, deaths, or other SAEs reported. No abnormal vital signs or abnormal physical findings were noted at the final examination on Day 8. No clinically significant changes were observed for clinical chemistry, hematology, and urinalysis parameters from Day 1 (baseline) to Day 8 and none of the abnormal clinical chemistry, hematology, or urinalysis values were assessed to be clinically significant.
L1779: Delgocitinib competitively inhibited JAK1, JAK2, JAK3, and TYK2 with respect to adenosine triphosphate. The K_i values for delgocitinib on JAK1, JAK2, JAK3, and TYK2 were 2.08, 1.71, 5.45, and 13.9 nmol/L, respectively. Furthermore, delgocitinib inhibited the JAKs activity of JAK1, JAK2, JAK3 and TYK2 with IC_{50} mean values of 2.8 nmol/L, 2.6 nmol/L, 12.5 nmol/L, and 57.8 nmol/L, respectively. The C_{max} in this maximal use study in subjects with CHE was 1.53 ng/mL (4.93 nmol/L) and 0.53 ng/mL (1.71 nmol/L) on Day 1 and 8, respectively. Overall, the C_{max} is on par with IC_{50} of JAK1 and JAK2. While the systemic concentration/IC_{50} ratio is much lower than oral JAK inhibitors, the systemic safety concern cannot be ruled out based on systemic exposure/IC_{50} comparison.
L1780: It is noted that approximately 33% of subjects on Day 1 and 20% subjects on Day 8 in the study showed concentrations in the subnanomolar range.
L1781: ## **Phase 1 Trial Under Maximal Use Conditions in Subjects With Moderate-to-Severe AD (LP0133-1181)**
L1782: ## Trial Objectives
L1783: To investigate safety and pharmacokinetics in adults, adolescents (12 to 17 years) and children (2 to 11 years) with moderate-to-severe AD.
L1784: ## Trial Design
L1785: This was a phase 1 open-label, multicenter, single-arm, 8-week trial in adults, adolescents, and children with moderate-to-severe AD.
L1786: The trial consisted of two parts:
L1787: - Part 1 enrolled adolescents (12 to 17 years) and adults (\ge18 years) only.
- Part 2 enrolled children (2 to 11 years).
L1788: Figure 16. Trial Design for Maximal Use Conditions in Subjects With AD, Trial LP0133-1181
L1789: Abbreviations: PK: pharmacokinetic; SFU: safety follow-up; N: number of subjects.
Source: Clinical trial report (LP0133-1181), Panel 3, Page No.19
L1790: Each part contained a screening period (Week 4 to Week 1), treatment period (Week 0 to Week 8) and a safety follow-up (Week 8 to Week 10). In Part 2 of the trial, the data monitoring committee assessed patient safety for children (age 2 to 11 years) during conduct.
L1791: ## Trial Population and Body Surface Area
L1792: The eligible subjects had a diagnosis with AD, with the severity of moderate-to-severe at screening and baseline according to the Validated Investigator's Global Assessment for Atopic Dermatitis (score \ge 3).
L1793: - Part 1: Adolescents (12 to 17 years; mean %BSA: 31.8 [range: 25.0 to 47.0]) and adults (\ge 18 years; mean %BSA of 33.6 [range: 29.0 to 50.0])
- Part 2: Children (2 to 11 years) with \ge 35\% treatable BSA at screening and a history of AD for \ge 3 months (children 2 to 5 years) and for \ge 12 months (children 6 to 11 years); mean %BSA of 46.0 (range: 30.0 to 78.0)
L1794: It is noted that the Applicant changed the inclusion criterion from 25 to 50% to \ge 35\% treatable BSA during the conduct of Part 2.
L1795: ## Formulation
L1796: The formulation of delgocitinib cream, 20 mg/g, that was used in the trial is not the final formulation. The difference in the formulation from the final formulation relates to the concentration of (b) (4) ((b) (4) in the early formulation is (b) (4) mg/g whereas the final formulation contained (b) (4) of (b) (4) mg/g).

L1807: Source: Panel 9, Page No.58 of 72, Clinical trial report (LP0133-1181)

L1809: Overall, the systemic exposure of delgocitinib tended to be higher in subjects with higher treatable %BSA involvement, with a more-than-proportional increase in AUC and C_{max} observed, compared with %BSA. It is noted that the systemic exposure in children 2- to 11-years-old was much higher than in adults and adolescents (Table 113) and this might be explained by a higher treatable %BSA (Table 112), a higher ratio of BSA/body weight, and the different disease severity (Table 112) compared with adults and adolescents.
L1810: The pharmacokinetic parameters C_{max}, AUC, and time to maximum concentration (T_{max}) values at Day 1 and Day 8 are presented in Table 113. The T_{max} values for the individual subjects ranged between 0.83 to 12.22 hours at Day 1 and from 0.00 to 12.03 hours at Day 8, indicating variability in the absorption profiles. However, for the majority of the subjects, the T_{max} was reached during the first hour after administration, with a median T_{max} of 1 to 2 hours in the different age groups. The mean exposure was low, but few subjects, mainly in the children group, showed higher exposure, with values for C_{max} ranging from 0.01 to 79.80 ng/mL at Day 1 and from 0.04 to 54.70 ng/mL at Day 8.
L1811: The reported AEs in the trial did not identify safety concerns for the subjects with higher exposure.

L1840: ## Phase 3 Trial in Adult Subjects With Moderate-to-Severe CHE (DELTA 2)
L1841: ## Trial Design
L1842: The Applicant conducted additional pharmacokinetic assessments of delgocitinib cream under maximal use conditions in a subset of subjects with moderate-to-severe CHE in the current phase 3 trial.
L1843: This trial was designed as randomized (2:1), double-blind, vehicle-controlled, parallel-group, multisite, phase 3 trial to assess the efficacy and safety of twice-daily delgocitinib cream compared with vehicle for 16 weeks in adult subjects with moderate-to-severe CHE.
L1844: Figure 22. Design for Phase 3 Trial (DELTA 2)
L1845: Source: Panel 3, Page No, 27 of 695, Clinical trial report LP0133-1402
L1846: Abbreviations: d, day; LTE, long-term extension; w, week, IGA-CHE, Investigator's Global Assessment for chronic hand eczema
L1847: ## Trial Population
L1848: The trial population was adults with moderate-to-severe CHE who had an inadequate response to, or for whom TCS were not advisable.
L1849: Out of the total number of subjects exposed to delgocitinib (N=313), 75 subjects had severe CHE (IGA-CHE score=4) at baseline; 19 of 75 subjects were in the upper quartile of IMP use. The mean IMP use was 0.98 g delgocitinib cream per application and mean %BSA was 2.17% in these 19 subjects and are considered to be under maximal use conditions. Remaining 294 subjects (238 subjects with moderate CHE and 56 subjects with severe disease but a lower IMP use) were not under MuST (mean IMP use of 0.52 g per application and mean %BSA of 1.48%).
L1850: The specific inclusion criteria included the following.
L1851: - Diagnosis of CHE, defined as hand eczema that has persisted for more than 3 months or returned twice or more within the last 12 months.
- Disease severity graded as moderate-to-severe at screening and baseline according to IGA-CHE (i.e., an IGA-CHE score of 3 or 4).
- HESD itch score (weekly average) of \ge 4 points at baseline. The baseline weekly average will be calculated from daily assessments of itch severity during the 7 days immediately preceding the baseline visit (Day 7 to Day 1). A minimum of 4 itch scores out of the 7 days is required to calculate the baseline average score.
- Subjects who have a documented recent history of inadequate response to treatment with TCS (at any time within 1 year before the screening visit) or for whom TCS are documented to be otherwise medically inadvisable (e.g., due to important side effects or safety risks).
L1852: ## NDA 219155
## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1853: ## Formulation
L1854: The final to-be-marketed formulation of delgocitinib cream, 20 mg/g, was used in the trial.
L1855: ## Pharmacokinetic Assessment
L1856: The pharmacokinetic blood samples were taken 2 to 6 hours post-application of IMP at Week 1, Week 4, and Week 16.

L1870: The mean plasma concentration of delgocitinib (one sample, 2 to 6 hours postmorning dose) under maximal use conditions was generally comparable between Week 1, 4, and 16 and was higher compared to the other population indicating that the degree of disease severity could impact the systemic exposure.
L1871: It is noted that approximately 62% of subjects at Week 1, 64% subjects at Week 4 and 81% subjects at Week 16 in the study showed concentrations in the subnanomolar range.
L1872: ## Phase 2a Trial in Adult Subjects With Chronic CHE (LP0133-1180)
L1873: ## Trial Design
L1874: This was a phase 2a, prospective, randomized, double-blind, two arms, vehicle-controlled, parallel-group, 8 weeks, phase 2a trial in subjects with CHE. A total of 91 male and female subjects with at least mild disease severity were randomized into this clinical trial, conducted across Germany at 13 clinical sites. The trial subjects were administered with twice-daily over a 56-day treatment period (8 weeks) to delgocitinib ointment 30 mg/g or vehicle (1.7 mg/cm2) in 2:1 ratio.
L1875: NDA 219155
ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1876: ## Trial Population
L1877: The subjects for the trial had the diagnosis of chronic CHE with:
L1878: - Clinical diagnosis of CHE with or without atopic etiology/background with a history of not adequately controlled disease activity with cutaneously applied corticosteroids.
- Physician Global Assessment of disease severity graded as at least mild at baseline (Week 0).
L1879: ## Formulation
L1880: The formulation of delgocitinib ointment, 30 mg/g that was used in the trial is different than the final formulation.
L1881: **Table 118. Delgocitinib Ointment Formulation Composition**
L1882: Name of component	Delgocitinib ointment
Vehicle	30 mg/g
Delgocitinib	-	30
L1883: a Contains the
L1884: (b) (4)
L1885: Source: Table 2-3, Quality Overall Summary, Page No. 10, Module: 2.3P
L1886: Abbreviation:
L1887: (b) (4)
L1888: ## Pharmacokinetic Assessment
L1889: Pharmacokinetic assessment was performed to evaluate systemic concentrations of delgocitinib at 1 week (2 to 6 hours after the morning application).
L1890: ## Pharmacokinetic Results
L1891: Out of 91 subjects, 86 (94.5%) subjects completed the trial (delgocitinib group: 59 [98.3%] subjects; vehicle group: 27 [87.1%] subjects). In the 60 samples obtained from subjects treated with delgocitinib ointment 30 mg/g, plasma concentrations above LLOQ of (5.00 pg/mL) were measured in all subjects. The measured concentrations in the 60 samples were between 5.01 and 2720 pg/mL with a mean value of 385 pg/mL. The systemic exposure of delgocitinib was low as very few samples were above 1 ng/mL.
L1892: **Figure 23. Plasma Concentrations of Delgocitinib in Subjects With Chronic Hand Eczema, Trial LP0133-1180**
L1893: Source: Figure 5, Page No. 137 of 366, Clinical trial report LP0133-1180
L1894: Delgocitinib was found to be more efficacious compared to vehicle as confirmed by Physician Global Assessment (45.7% versus 14.9%) at Week 8. The improvement in the disease severity seen for both secondary measures HECI assessment and Patient's Global Assessment (PaGA) of disease severity supports this conclusion, showing a lower mean HECI score (12.3 versus 27.9) and a greater percentage of subjects achieving treatment success according to PaGA (31.0% versus 17.6%) in the delgocitinib group than the vehicle group at Week 8.
L1895: The most common AEs reported for the delgocitinib group included nasopharyngitis (25.0%), postoperative wound infection and headache (6.7%, each), as well as hand dermatitis (5.0%). The only AEs reported more frequently in the delgocitinib group than in the vehicle group were nasopharyngitis and headache. An AE of worsening of CHE was reported more frequently in the vehicle group than in the delgocitinib group.
L1896: ## Phase 2b Dose-Ranging Trial in Adult Subjects With Chronic CHE (LP0133-1273)
L1897: ## Trial Design
L1898: This was a phase 2b, double-blind, randomized, five-arm, vehicle-controlled, parallel-group, multicenter, dose-ranging trial to establish a dose-response relationship and to investigate the efficacy and safety of delgocitinib cream in the treatment of adult subjects with mild-to-severe CHE. A total of 250 subjects were randomized in a 1:1:1:1:1 ratio (delgocitinib cream 1, 3, 8, or 20 mg/g or placebo).
L1899: The randomization was stratified by the severity of CHE according to the Investigator's Global Assessment for CHE (IGA-CHE score of 2 [mild], 3 [moderate], or 4 [severe]) and region (Europe and North America).
L1900: ## Trial Population
L1901: The subjects for the trial had chronic CHE with:
L1902: - Diagnosis of chronic hand eczema defined as hand eczema that has persisted for more than 3 months or returned twice or more within the last 12 months.
- Disease severity graded as mild-to-severe according to Investigator's Global Assessment for chronic hand eczema (i.e., an IGA-CHE score of 2 or more).
- Recent history (within 1 year before the screening visit) of inadequate response to topical corticosteroid treatment or topical corticosteroid treatment being medically inadvisable.
L1903: ## Formulation
L1904: Delgocitinib cream at 1, 3, 8, or 20 mg/g or placebo were administered twice daily via cutaneous application for 16 weeks.
L1905: The applications were performed approximately 12 hours apart. The formulation of delgocitinib (20-mg/g cream) used in the trial is different than the final to-be-marketed formulation in terms of the (b) (4) concentration (similar for the formulation used in MuST in AD subjects; Trial LP0133-1181), where the change in the formulation was deemed minor.
L1906: ## Pharmacokinetic Assessment
L1907: One blood sample from each subject was collected for pharmacokinetic assessments 2 to 6 hours after the morning application of IMP.
L1908: ## Pharmacokinetic/Pharmacodynamic Assessment
L1909: Skin swabs were collected on Day 1 and Week 16 to investigate the effect of treatment with delgocitinib on the skin microbiome in general and on colonization with *Staphylococcus aureus*. These results will not be described in detail as this pharmacodynamic assessment is not considered relevant to this program.
L1910: ## Pharmacokinetic Results
L1911: Delgocitinib showed low systemic exposure across all doses, although with a tendency to increase with dose (geometric mean range: 0.02 to 0.26 ng/mL), as shown below.

L1918: ## Phase 1 QT Prolongation and Proarrhythmic Trial in Healthy Subjects (LP0133-1409)
L1919: ## Trial Design
L1920: Randomized, phase 1, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel-group, single-dose, single-center trial to evaluate QTcF prolongation and proarrhythmic potential of delgocitinib following oral administration in healthy subjects.
L1921: The trial consisted of a screening (between 28 and 2 days before IMP administration), an in-clinic treatment period (Days 1 to 2), and a safety follow-up phone call 2 weeks after dosing. Dosing was done on Day 1 and PK blood sampling and 12-lead ECG monitoring were performed from predose until 24 hours postdose.
L1922: The trial subjects received a single oral dose at one-dose level. The trial included four-dose cohorts; 6- and 12-mg dose levels were dosed in Part 1 and following evaluation of Part 1 data, the planned dose levels of 1.5 and 3 mg delgocitinib were administered in Part 2.
L1923: The trial consisted of four-dose cohorts in two parts, as shown below (Figure 25).
L1924: **Figure 25. Design for Phase 1 QT Prolongation and Proarrhythmic, Trial LP0133-1409**
L1925: Source: Panel 2, Page 14 of 246, Clinical trial report LP0133-1409
L1926: Abbreviations: C_{max}, maximum plasma concentration; ECG, electrocardiogram; N, number of subjects in cohort; PK, pharmacokinetic
L1927: ## Formulation
L1928: Delgocitinib capsules of 1.5 mg, 3 mg, 6 mg, and 12 mg were administered as a single dose in the morning of Day 1 under fasted condition.
L1929: ## Pharmacokinetic Assessment
L1930: Predose (-30 min), 20, 40 minutes, 1 hour, 1 hour and 20 minutes, 1 hour and 40 minutes, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12 hours and 24-hour postdose after single dosing on Day 1 (up to three additional PK blood samples may have been taken from each subject).
L1931: ## Pharmacokinetic Results
L1932: Following single-ascending oral doses of 1.5- to 12-mg delgocitinib, a rapid absorption phase was observed, with a median T_{max} of 0.83 to 1.00 hour. Delgocitinib profile declined in a multiphasic manner, post-C_{max}. The elimination half-life was below 3 hours in all dose groups (2.02 to 2.85 hours), with individual subject values not exceeding 5 hours.
L1933: Geometric mean AUC_{0-\infty} was approximately 10.3-fold higher following the 8-fold increase in dose from 1.5- to 12-mg delgocitinib, and geometric mean C_{max} was approximately 13.8-fold higher following the 8-fold increase in the delgocitinib dose.

L1941: ## QT Prolongation Potential (Safety ECGs)
L1942: No clinically relevant changes from baseline in the 12-lead ECG parameters were observed measured and no clinically significant abnormalities in the morphology of the 12-lead ECG for any subject. None of the subjects showed postdose QTcF &gt;450 msec. A single subject had a postdose increase from baseline in QTcF &gt;30 msec following a single 3-mg dose of delgocitinib. This subject had an increase of 33 msec on Day 1, 1-hour postdose, at which time the investigator considered the ECG to be abnormal, not clinically significant.
L1943: The estimated population slope of the concentration-QTc relationship was 0.016 msec per ng/mL (90% CI: -0.0124 to 0.0442) with a treatment effect-specific intercept of 1.26 msec (90% CI: -0.971 to 3.482). Neither the treatment effect-specific intercept (p-value of 0.3444) nor the slope (p-value of 0.3405) were statistically significant at the 10% level.
L1944: Based on the consult response from IRT QT team, the data did not suggest that delgocitinib is associated with large mean QTcF interval prolongation and the results of the primary analysis are further supported by the lack of QTc prolongation in by-time analysis and categorical analysis at the highest studied dose of 12 mg (oral) that covers the maximum anticipated therapeutic exposure (C_{max}) by 193-fold. The QT team also provided language for the labelling.
L1945: ## Phase 1 Oral Administration, Single and Multiple Doses (Study NBX1-1)
L1946: ## Trial Design
L1947: The trial was designed to investigate the safety and pharmacokinetics of orally administered delgocitinib in healthy Japanese male adult volunteers and designed as a placebo-controlled, single-blind trial. In addition, pharmacokinetics of delgocitinib administered under fasted and fed conditions was investigated to determine the food effect.
L1948: Part 1 is designed as randomized, single-blind, single-dose, placebo-controlled trial with delgocitinib doses of 1 mg, 5 mg, 25 mg, 50 mg, 100 mg, or placebo (fasted) (N=40), whereas Part 2 is designed as a randomized, single-blind, multiple-dose, placebo-controlled trial; it consists of three steps; under steps 2-1 and 2-2, delgocitinib administered as multiple doses once daily after standard breakfast for 14 days. In step 2-3, delgocitinib was administered as multiple doses twice daily after standard breakfast and dinner for 14 days (on Day 14, after breakfast only).
L1949: ## Pharmacokinetic Assessments (Plasma)
L1950: - Part 1: Predose, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 36, 48 hours postdose
- Part 2: Predose, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 36, 48 hours postdose, 0 hour after dosing on Day 2, 3, 7, and 10 and on Day 14 at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, 36, 48 hours
L1951: ## Pharmacokinetic Assessments (Urine)
L1952: - Part 1: Predose, 0 to 6, 6 to 12 and 12 to 24 hours, 24 to 36 and 36 to 48 hours
- Part 2: Predose, 0 to 6, 6 to 12 and 12 to 24 hours and 24 to 36 hours
L1953: ## Pharmacokinetic Results
L1954: After a single-dose administration of delgocitinib at the above doses under fasted conditions, the T_{max} was 0.75, 1.00, 1.00, 1.00, and 1.00 hour, respectively, and the C_{max} was 11.0, 56.5, 313, 619, and 956 ng/mL, respectively. After the T_{max}, the plasma concentrations of delgocitinib decreased in a biphasic manner. In the 5, 25, 50, and 100-mg groups, the t_{1/2} was 2.71, 3.82, 3.96, and 4.45 hours, respectively, the AUC_{inf} was 175, 957, 1815, and 3212 ng hr/mL, respectively, the apparent clearance at steady-state was 28.80, 26.40, 28.35, and 31.16 L/hour, respectively,
L1955: NDA 219155
L1956: ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L1957: and the apparent volume of distribution during terminal phase was 112.7, 147.5, 164.0, and 199.7 L, respectively.

L1978: Abbreviations: AUC_{tau}, area under the concentration-time curve during the dosing interval; BID, twice daily; CL_F, apparent clearance at steady-state; C_{max}, maximum plasma concentration; JTE-052, delgocitinib; N, number of subjects; NA, not available; QD, once daily; t_{1/2}, half-life; t_{max}, time to maximum concentration; V_{z_F}, apparent volume of distribution during terminal phase
L1979: In all the treatment groups, the administered oral dose of delgocitinib was mostly excreted into urine. Thus, the oral bioavailability of delgocitinib is expected to be high and delgocitinib was primarily eliminated as unchanged compound in the urine (approximately 75% of the total dose was found as unchanged compound in urine).
L1980: It was considered that there was no accumulation of delgocitinib following once-daily or twice-daily multiple-dose administration of delgocitinib for 14 days.
L1981: ## Mass Balance Trial
L1982: The Applicant did not conduct a mass balance trial in the current NDA for delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g and provided the following justification:
L1983: - The Applicant stated that the systemic exposure of delgocitinib after topical administration was consistently observed to be low across trials including the studies with subjects treated under maximal use conditions.
- The bioavailability of delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g under maximal use conditions in subjects with CHE was found to be low (approx. 0.5 to 0.6% versus oral).The mean relative bioavailability of delgocitinib cream across all oral doses is 0.6%.
- The calculated relative bioavailability for delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g (2285) compared to the different oral doses in Trial NBX1-1 and Trial 1409 is presented below (Table 124).
- The low bioavailability and topical administration regimen make it difficult to obtain reliable results from absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion studies (mass balance trial) due to high variability associated with recovery.
- Low metabolic turnover of delgocitinib (in vitro as well as clinical data from Trial NBX1-1), about approximately 75% of the total drug dose is excreted unchanged in urine and levels of four main metabolites were low (approximately 0.8%, 0.4%, 0.7%, and 1.7% of the average delgocitinib).

L1989: Therefore, based on low systemic exposure, low metabolic turnover, and primary excretion route being unchanged drug in the urine, the Applicant concluded that mass balance trial is not warranted.
L1990: The Applicant's justifications for not conducting mass balance trial are reasonable.
L1991: ## Pharmacodynamic Assessments
L1992: The Applicant assessed skin colonization with *S. aureus* in Study 1273. This pharmacodynamic assessment will not be described further as it is not relevant to the mode of the proposed treatment. Pharmacodynamic assessments also included assessment of gene expression that evaluated inflammatory and skin barrier integrity markers. This pharmacodynamic evaluation is considered as exploratory, and it will also not be included in this review.

L2026: daily applications of delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g compared with cream vehicle in the treatment of adult subjects with moderate to severe CHE.	participating in the LTE trial) per subject.	
1) The IGA-CHE is an instrument used in clinical trials to rate the severity of the subject's global CHE and is based on a 5-point scale ranging from 0 (clear) to 4 (severe).
2) The HECSI is an instrument used in clinical trials to rate the severity of 6 clinical signs (erythema, infiltration/papulation, vesicles, fissures, scaling, and oedema) and the extent of the lesions in each of the 5 hand regions (fingertips, fingers [except fingertips], palm of hands, back of hands, and wrists) by use of standard scales. The HECSI score will range from 0 (lowest possible score) to 360 (highest possible score).
3) The HESD is an eDiary in which subjects will assess the worst severity of 6 individual signs and symptoms of CHE (itch, pain, cracking, redness, dryness, flaking) over the past 24 hours using an 11-point numeric rating scale throughout the trial on a daily basis.
4) Among subjects with a baseline HESD itch score (weekly average) \ge4 points.
5) Among subjects with a baseline HESD score (weekly average) \ge4 points.
6) Among subjects with a baseline HESD pain score (weekly average) \ge4 points.
7) Among subjects with a baseline DLQI score \ge4 points.
8) The HEIS addresses 9 items within the following domains: PDAL, embarrassment with the appearance of the hands, frustration with CHE, sleep, work, and physical functioning. Each item is scored on a 5-point scale ranging from 0 (not at all) to 4 (extremely).
9) An event will be considered treatment emergent if started after the first application of IMP or if started before the first application of IMP and worsened in severity after first dose of IMP.
Abbreviations: CHE = chronic hand eczema; DLQI = Dermatology Life Quality Index; HECSI = Hand Eczema Severity Index; HECSI-75 = at least 75% improvement in HECSI score from baseline; HECSI-90 = at least 90% improvement in HECSI score from baseline; HEIS = Hand Eczema Impact Scale; HESD = Hand Eczema Symptom Diary; IGA-CHE = Investigator's Global Assessment for chronic hand eczema; IGA-CHE TS = IGA-CHE treatment success, i.e. an IGA-CHE score of 0 (clear) or 1 (almost clear) with a \ge2 step improvement from baseline; IMP = investigational medicinal product; PDAL = Proximal Daily Activity Limitations.

Final collection of data for the primary endpoint	Week 16.
Trial design	
The trial is a phase 3 randomised, double-blind, vehicle-controlled, parallel-group, multi-site trial in which adult subjects with moderate to severe CHE will be treated with delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g or cream vehicle for 16 weeks.
The trial consists of a screening period, a treatment period, and a follow-up period.
Screening period
The screening period has a minimum duration of 1 week and a maximum duration of 4 weeks. At the screening visit, the subjects' eligibility to enter the trial will be checked, and wash-out of treatments listed as exclusion criteria will be initiated if applicable. In view of a 28-day wash out for some of these treatments, the screening period can be extended up to 31 days. The subjects will receive an eDiary device and training in completion of the eDiary.
Treatment period
At baseline (Day 1), the subjects' eligibility to enter the trial will be confirmed. If still eligible, the subjects will be randomised 2:1 to treatment with delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g or cream vehicle. The randomisation will be stratified by the baseline severity of CHE according to IGA-CHE (moderate and severe) and region (Europe and North America).
L2027: Diagnosis of CHE, defined as hand eczema that has persisted for more than 3 months or returned twice or more within the last 12 months.
Disease severity graded as moderate to severe at screening and baseline according to IGA-CHE (i.e. an IGA-CHE score of 3 or 4).
HESD itch score (weekly average) of ≥4 points at baseline.
Subjects who have a documented recent history of inadequate response to treatment with TCS (at any time within 1 year before the screening visit) or for whom TCS are documented to be otherwise medically inadvisable (e.g. due to important side effects or safety risks).
Subjects adherent to standard non-medicated skin care including avoidance of known and relevant irritants and allergens.

Main criteria for exclusion	Concurrent skin diseases on the hands, e.g. tinea manuum.
Active AD requiring medical treatment in regions other than the hands and feet.
Active psoriasis on any part of the body.
Hyperkeratotic hand eczema in combination with a history of psoriasis on any part of the body.
Clinically significant infection (e.g. impetiginised hand eczema) on the hands.
Systemic treatment with immunosuppressive drugs (e.g. methotrexate, cyclosporine, azathioprine), immunomodulating drugs, retinoids (e.g. allitretinoin), or corticosteroids within 28 days prior to baseline (steroid eyedrops and inhaled or intranasal steroids corresponding to up to 1 mg prednisolone for allergic conjunctivitis, asthma, or rhinitis are allowed).
Use of tanning beds, phototherapy (e.g. UVB, UVA1, PUVA), or bleach baths on the hands within 28 days prior to baseline.
Previous or current treatment with JAK inhibitors (including delgocitinib/LEO 124249), systemic or topical.
Cutaneously applied treatment with immunomodulators (e.g. PDE-4 inhibitors, pimecrolimus, tacrolimus) or TCS on the hands within 14 days prior to baseline.
Use of systemic antibiotics or cutaneously applied antibiotics on the hands within 14 days prior to baseline.
Other transdermal or cutaneously applied therapy on the hands (except for the use of subject's own emollients) within 7 days prior to baseline.
Cutaneously applied treatments in regions other than the hands, which could interfere with clinical trial evaluations or pose a safety concern within 7 days prior to baseline.
Treatment with any marketed biological therapy or investigational biologic agents (including immunoglobulin, anti-IgE, and dupilumab):Any cell-depleting agents including but not limited to rituximab: within 6 months prior to baseline, or until lymphocyte count returns to normal, whichever is longer.
Other biologics: within 3 months or 5 half-lives, whichever is longer, prior to baseline.

Clinically significant infection within 28 days prior to baseline which, in the opinion of the investigator, may compromise the safety of the subject in the trial, interfere with evaluation of the IMP, or reduce the subject's ability to participate in the trial. Clinically significant infections are defined as:A systemic infection.
A serious skin infection requiring parenteral (intravenous or intramuscular) antibiotics, antiviral, or antifungal medication.
L2028: ## NDA 219155
## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L2029: History of any known primary immunodeficiency disorder including a positive HIV test at screening, or the subject taking antiretroviral medications as determined by medical history and/or subject's verbal report.
Any disorder which is not stable and could:Affect the safety of the subject throughout the trial.
Impede the subject's ability to complete the trial.

Examples include but are not limited to cardiovascular, gastrointestinal, hepatic, renal, neurological, musculoskeletal, infectious, endocrine, metabolic, haematological, immunological, and psychiatric disorders, and major physical impairment.
Positive hepatitis B surface antigen or hepatitis C virus antibody serology at screening.

Investigational medicinal product	Name of IMP: delgocitinib cream.
Active substance: delgocitinib.
Dosage form: cream.
Concentration: 20 mg/g and cream vehicle.
Dose and method of administration: topical application twice daily.

Duration of trial participation	The duration of trial participation will be up to 22 weeks, consisting of a screening period of up to 4 weeks, a treatment period of 16 weeks, and a safety follow-up period of 2 weeks (safety follow-up period not applicable for subjects participating in the LTE trial).
Number of subjects	A total of 470 eligible subjects will be randomised in a 2:1 ratio to delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g or cream vehicle.
Number and distribution of trial sites	Approximately 55 sites in Europe and Canada.
Statistical methods	
For the primary and key secondary endpoints, confirmatory one-sided (superiority) hypotheses will be tested for delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g vs. cream vehicle based on the primary analysis for the primary estimand. A closed testing procedure with hierarchical tests, alpha splitting, and alpha recycling will be used to control the overall type I error at a nominal one-sided 2.5% level. The statistical testing strategy is built on the principle that the IGA-CHE TS superiority at Week 16 will have to be established before testing for additional benefits (key secondary endpoints) related to efficacy and health-related quality of life.
The primary estimand for the primary and key secondary endpoints will use a 'composite' strategy where the occurrence of pre-defined IEs (initiation of rescue treatment and permanent discontinuation of IMP) is a component of the endpoint. A first supplementary estimand will use a 'pandemic modified composite' strategy which follows a 'composite' strategy for IEs independent of the COVID-19 pandemic, attempting to quantify the effect of the randomised treatment in a world without COVID-19 pandemic. A second supplementary estimand will use a 'treatment policy' strategy which attempts to quantify the effect of the randomised treatment regardless of whether an IE occurs. The second supplementary estimand will be used for endpoints related to Week 16 (primary endpoint visit).
The primary analysis for the primary estimand for the binary endpoints will be CMH test stratified by region and baseline IGA-CHE score. The primary analysis for the primary estimand for the continuous endpoints will be ANCOVA with effects of treatment group, region, baseline IGA-CHE score, and baseline value (endpoint of interest).

Innovaderm Research Inc., Montreal (Quebec), Canada
Sponsor	LEO Pharma A/S, Industriparken 55, DK-2750 Ballerup, Denmark

6) Among subjects with a baseline HESD pain score (weekly average) ≥4 points.
7) Among subjects with a baseline DLQI score ≥4 points.
8) The HEIS addresses 9 items within the following domains: PDAL, embarrassment with the appearance of the hands, frustration with CHE, sleep, work, and physical functioning. Each item is scored on a 5-point scale ranging from 0 (not at all) to 4 (extremely).
9) An event will be considered treatment-emergent if started after the first application of IMP or if started before the first application of IMP and worsened in severity after first dose of IMP.
Abbreviations: CHE = chronic hand eczema; DLQI = Dermatology Life Quality Index; HECSI-75 = at least 75% improvement in HECSI score from baseline; HECSI-90 = at least 90% improvement in HECSI score from baseline; HECSI = Hand Eczema Severity Index; HEIS = Hand Eczema Impact Scale; HESD = Hand Eczema Symptom Diary; IGA-CHE = Investigator's Global Assessment for chronic hand eczema; IGA-CHE TS = IGA-CHE treatment success, i.e., an IGA-CHE score of 0 (clear) or 1 (almost clear) with a ≥2 step improvement from baseline; IMP = investigational medicinal product;; PDAL = Proximal Daily Activity Limitations.

Final collection of data for the primary endpoint	Week 16.
Trial design	
The trial is a phase 3 randomised, double-blind, vehicle-controlled, parallel-group, multisite trial in which adult subjects with moderate to severe CHE will be treated with delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g or cream vehicle for 16 weeks.
The trial consists of a screening period, a treatment period, and a follow-up period.
Screening period
The screening period has a minimum duration of 1 week and a maximum duration of 4 weeks. At the screening visit, the subjects' eligibility to enter the trial will be checked, and wash-out of treatments listed as exclusion criteria will be initiated if applicable. In view of a 28-day wash out for some of these treatments, the screening period can be extended up to 31 days. The subjects will receive an eDiary device and training in completion of the eDiary.
Treatment period
At baseline (Day 1), the subjects' eligibility to enter the trial will be confirmed. If still eligible, the subjects will be randomised 2:1 to treatment with delgocitinib cream 20 mg/g or cream vehicle. The randomisation will be stratified by the baseline severity of CHE according to IGA-CHE (moderate and severe) and region (Europe and North America).
The first application of IMP will occur at the trial site at baseline (Day 1). The subsequent IMP applications will be performed by the subjects at home twice
L2039: daily for 16 weeks. During the treatment period, the subjects will be required to return to the trial site for the visits scheduled at Weeks 1, 2, 4, 8, 12, and 16. The last IMP application will occur at the subject's home before the subject attends the visit scheduled at Week 16.
Follow-up period
The subjects will attend a follow-up visit (performed via phone, but can be a site visit if needed) approximately 2 weeks after the last IMP application for assessment of safety. Eligible subjects who complete the treatment period (i.e., who do not permanently discontinue IMP prior to Week 16) will be invited to participate in an open-label LTE trial (LP0133-1403, conducted under a separate protocol). Subjects who continue in the LTE trial will not be required to complete the follow-up period, as collection of safety data and safety surveillance will continue in the LTE trial.

* For subjects using treatments that are listed as exclusion criteria, the duration of the screening period will depend on the required wash-out period as defined in Section 8.3. In view of a 28-day wash-out for some of these treatments, the screening period can be extended up to 31 days.
Abbreviations: d = day; IGA-CHE = Investigator's Global Assessment for chronic hand eczema; LTE = long-term extension; N = number of subjects; w = week.

Main assessments

IGA-CHE for assessment of disease severity.

Subject assessments of efficacy and health-related quality of life; PROs:

Dermatology Life Quality Index (DLQI).
Safety assessments:
Vital signs, physical examination, ECG, laboratory testing, subject assessment of local tolerability, and AE reporting.

Main criteria for inclusion	Age 18 years or above at screening.
Diagnosis of CHE, defined as hand eczema that has persisted for more than 3 months or returned twice or more within the last 12 months.

L2040: Subjects who have a documented recent history of inadequate response to treatment with TCS (at any time within 1 year before the screening visit) or for whom TCS are documented to be otherwise medically inadvisable (e.g., due to important side effects or safety risks).
Subjects adherent to standard nonmedicated skin care including avoidance of known and relevant irritants and allergens.

Main criteria for exclusion	Concurrent skin diseases on the hands, e.g., tinea manuum.
Active AD requiring medical treatment in regions other than the hands and feet.
Active psoriasis on any part of the body.
Hyperkeratotic hand eczema in combination with a history of psoriasis on any part of the body.
Clinically significant infection (e.g., impetiginised hand eczema) on the hands.
Systemic treatment with immunosuppressive drugs (e.g., methotrexate, cyclosporine, azathioprine), immunomodulating drugs, retinoids (e.g., alitretinoin), or corticosteroids within 28 days prior to baseline (steroid eyedrops and inhaled or intranasal steroids corresponding to up to 1 mg prednisolone for allergic conjunctivitis, asthma, or rhinitis are allowed).
Use of tanning beds, phototherapy (e.g., UVB, UVA1, PUVA), or bleach baths on the hands within 28 days prior to baseline.
Previous or current treatment with JAK inhibitors (including delgocitinib/LEO 124249), systemic or topical.
Cutaneously applied treatment with immunomodulators (e.g., PDE-4 inhibitors, pimecrolimus, tacrolimus) or TCS on the hands within 14 days prior to baseline.
Use of systemic antibiotics or cutaneously applied antibiotics on the hands within 14 days prior to baseline.
Other transdermal or cutaneously applied therapy on the hands (except for the use of subject's own emollients) within 7 days prior to baseline.
Cutaneously applied treatments in regions other than the hands, which could interfere with clinical trial evaluations or pose a safety concern within 7 days prior to baseline.
Treatment with any marketed biological therapy or investigational biologic agents (including immunoglobulin, anti-IgE, and dupilumab):Any cell-depleting agents including but not limited to rituximab: within 6 months prior to baseline, or until lymphocyte count returns to normal, whichever is longer.
Other biologics: within 3 months or 5 half-lives, whichever is longer, prior to baseline.

Clinically significant infection within 28 days prior to baseline which, in the opinion of the investigator, may compromise the safety of the subject in the trial, interfere with evaluation of the IMP, or reduce the subject's ability to participate in the trial. Clinically significant infections are defined as:A systemic infection.
A serious skin infection requiring parenteral (intravenous or intramuscular) antibiotics, antiviral, or antifungal medication.

History of any known primary immunodeficiency disorder including a positive HIV test at screening, or the subject taking antiretroviral medications as determined by medical history and/or subject's verbal report.
Any disorder which is not stable and could:Affect the safety of the subject throughout the trial.

## ANZUPGO (delgocitinib) cream, 2%
L2042: Impede the subject's ability to complete the trial.
(1) Examples include but are not limited to cardiovascular, gastrointestinal, hepatic, renal, neurological, musculoskeletal, infectious, endocrine, metabolic, haematological, immunological, and psychiatric disorders, and major physical impairment.
